- [x] **M0134-0130 — inet.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **PARKED 2026-08-24, contained fix shipped** (0% parity, diff 1397→1298 lines). Root cause: `inet`/`cidr` columns had zero input canonicalization or output formatting — a literal like `'10'::cidr` stored/re-emitted verbatim, never expanded to PG's canonical `10.0.0.0/8`, and cidr's "bits set to right of mask" validation didn't exist at all (`'192.168.1.2/30'` silently accepted instead of raising `22P02`). Added `normalizeInetCidrText`/`formatInetAddr`/`formatInetV4`/`formatInetV6` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`), a faithful Go port of PG's `network_in` (postgres/src/backend/utils/adt/network.c) + `pg_inet_net_ntop` (postgres/src/port/inet_net_ntop.c), reusing the existing btree-key parser (`parseInetKeyText`/`cidrDefaultV4Mask`/`maskInetAddr`, M0134-0002 C5). Go's `net.IP.String()` could not be reused for IPv6 formatting: PG's embedded-dotted-decimal-tail rule fires for any 6-word leading zero run, not just the well-known `::ffff:a.b.c.d` v4-mapped form Go recognizes — confirmed live Go collapses `::ffff:1.2.3.4` to bare `1.2.3.4` and renders `::4.3.2.1` as `::403:201` (pure hex groups), both wrong for PG. Wired into `coerceTextLikeDatum` (`internal/executor/codec.go`), the box/circle canonicalize-on-assignment chokepoint (M0134-0094/-0098) — deliberately not into `evalCast`, matching that precedent (explicit `::inet`/`::cidr` casts on bare constants stay unvalidated, same as box/circle). New test `TestNormalizeInetCidrText` (`internal/executor/inet_cidr_normalize_test.go`, 14 subtests). Design `docs/design/m0134-0130-inet-cidr-normalization.md`, indexed in README.md. - [x] **M0134-0131 — infinite_recurse.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **PASS 2026-08-24** (100% parity, `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh --verbose infinite_recurse`). Root cause: `create function infinite_recurse() returns int as 'select infinite_recurse()' language sql;` then `select infinite_recurse();` recursed unbounded through `evalStoredRoutineFuncCall` → `executeSQLRoutine` → `optimizer.Plan`/`Build` → `evalFuncCall` → back to `evalStoredRoutineFuncCall`, smashing the Go goroutine stack — a Go "stack overflow" is a FATAL, unrecoverable process crash (not a catchable panic), so it took the whole server down and the client saw "server closed the connection unexpectedly" instead of PG's `54001 stack depth limit exceeded` (`postgres/src/backend/utils/misc/stack_depth.c` `check_stack_depth`, which polls the C stack pointer against `max_stack_depth` on every function call — goopg has no C-stack-pointer equivalent). Fix: added `Context.RoutineDepth` (`internal/executor/context.go`), incremented/checked/decremented around every `executeStoredRoutine` call (`internal/executor/plpgsql_runtime.go`) — covers both LANGUAGE sql and plpgsql since both build their child Context via `*child = *ctx`, carrying the running depth forward by value at every recursion level like a real stack depth would be. Capped at `maxRoutineCallDepth=2000` (comfortably above any legitimate recursion depth in the regress suite, well below Go's default 1GB max goroutine stack); raises the same `54001`/"stack depth limit exceeded" PG emits. - [x] **M0134-0133 — json_encoding.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **PARKED 2026-08-24, contained fix shipped** (0% parity → `^+ERROR` 0, no new false positives). Root cause: `tryTypedLiteral` (`internal/parser/select.go`)'s SQL-standard `typename 'literal'` grammar whitelist never included `json`/`jsonb` (a parser-wide gap, not file-local — added incrementally per-fixture since M0097-0003), so `json '{"a": 1}' -> 'a'` fell through to `parseColumnOrCall` and hit a hard "syntax error at or near '{\"a\": 1}'" on nearly every statement in the file. Added `"json", "jsonb"` to the whitelist plus two new `evalTypedStringLit` arms (`internal/executor/expr.go`) reusing `canonicalizeJSONB`/`validateJSONText` — the SAME validators `::json`/`::jsonb` CASTs (M0134-0120) and jsonb/json column coercion already share — making the typed literal a third twin of the same input boundary (Rule #2). Every syntax-error line is now gone; the case is not a full pass because 12 remaining lines expose a distinct REFACTOR-tier gap: Go's `encoding/json` (which the shared validators delegate `\uXXXX` decoding to) neither enforces PG's strict UTF-16 surrogate-pair lexing (`json_lex_string`, silently emits U+FFFD for unpaired/misordered surrogates instead of raising `22P02`) nor rejects U+0000 when a value is unescaped into `text` (jsonb assembly, `->>`) the way PG's "cannot be converted to text" check does — needs a hand-ported JSON string-escape lexer plus a "converting to text" flag threaded through `->`/`->>`/jsonb assembly. New tests `TestParseJSONTypedLiteral`/`TestParseJSONTypedLiteralWithOperator` (`internal/parser/json_typed_literal_test.go`), `TestJSONTypedStringLitMatchesCast` (`internal/executor/jsonb_canonical_test.go`). - [x] **M0134-0135 — largeobject.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **PARKED 2026-08-24, no CONTAINED bucket, no code shipped** (0% parity at HEAD, sized live). Every one of the file's 40+ `lo_*`/`loread`/`lowrite` calls fails "function ... does not exist" (or a downstream cascade thereof): `pg_proc` seeds every `be_lo_*` handler name (`internal/initdb/pg_proc_seed_data.go:407-2316`) but `evalFuncCall` never recognizes any of them — real PG's entire large-object facility (`postgres/src/backend/libpq/be-fsstubs.c`) is missing wholesale in goopg: `pg_largeobject`/`pg_largeobject_metadata` chunked catalog storage, a per-backend per-transaction descriptor table backing `lo_open`/`lo_close`/`lo_lseek[64]`/`lo_tell[64]`/`loread`/`lowrite`/`lo_truncate[64]` (the file's dominant idiom is `UPDATE ... SET fd = lo_open(...)` then reusing `fd` across later statements in the SAME transaction), `\lo_import`/`\lo_export`/`\lo_list`/`\lo_unlink`/`\dl`/`:LASTOID` psql meta-commands, `ALTER LARGE OBJECT OWNER`/`GRANT ON LARGE OBJECT`/`COMMENT ON LARGE OBJECT` DDL forms, and large-object-specific read-only-transaction write rejection. This is a self-contained storage subsystem, REFACTOR-tier, own milestone — unlike every prior M0134 case there is no case-local bug to ship as a contained fix (matches the M0134-0038 `json.sql` precedent: sizing-only, no shipped fix). Secondary finding, NOT new: `SELECT lo_open(2121, x'40000'::int)` additionally surfaces the already-ledgered M0134-0092 gap (untyped `x'...'` bit-string literals decode to a plain `StringConst` of binary-digit text, so `::int` tries decimal-parsing the digits instead of dispatching PG's `bittoint4` raw-bits reinterpretation, `postgres/src/backend/utils/adt/varbit.c:1585-1607`) — no safe file-local fix exists (a bit-literal StringConst is indistinguishable from a real string literal at the Datum level; the real fix is giving bit literals a proper BITOID type, M0134-0092's resume point). - [x] **M0134-0140 — maintain_every.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **CLOSED 2026-08-24, real fix landed, 100% parity**. Root cause: `pg_class.relhassubclass` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go`, virtual pg_class builder) only checked `partitionChildren[oid]`, never `inheritanceChildren[oid]` — a plain `CREATE TABLE ... INHERITS (parent)` never flipped the parent's `relhassubclass` to `t` at all. Fixing that surfaced a second, independent bug: `DropTable` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:20995`) never unlinked the dropped child's OID from its parent's `inheritanceChildren`/`partitionChildren` list, so `relhassubclass` went stale `t` forever after `DROP TABLE <child>` — PG's `RemoveInheritance` deletes the `pg_inherits` row as part of the drop's dependency scan; goopg has no `pg_inherits` heap, so the equivalent fix is unlinking the in-memory edge in `DropTable` itself. Fixed both: (1) OR'd `inheritanceChildren[t.OID]` into the `relhassubclass` computation, (2) added an unlink block in `DropTable` for both `InheritsParentOIDs` (classic inheritance) and a full sweep of `partitionChildren` by value (covers the ATTACH PARTITION case where `Table.PartitionParentOID` stays 0, mirroring the existing `PartitionParentOf` fallback pattern). No new test file — `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh maintain_every` itself is the regression coverage (CSV pass_required=yes locks it in). Confirmed no regression via git-stash-diff (inherit.sql's pre-existing 3300-line failure is byte-identical with/without this change, and its diff has zero `relhassubclass` occurrences) plus a live re-check of alter_table/create_table_like/partition_join/partition_prune (all pre-existing failures, none mention `relhassubclass`). CSV row flipped `not-tried` → `pass`, `pass_required` → `yes`, via `make regen-testport`. No deferral-ledger row — this is a genuine full fix, not a shortcut or parked partial. Gates: `go build ./...` PASS; `go test ./internal/catalog/... ./internal/executor/...` PASS; `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh --verbose maintain_every` 100% parity (was 0%, 15-line diff); `make regen-testport`/`make check-testport-inventory` PASS. CASCADE`'s `DETAIL` lines omit the schema qualifier on `drop cascades to function <name>` and cascade-notice ordering doesn't match PG's dependency-walk order. - [x] **M0134-0152 — polymorphism.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **PARKED 2026-08-25, sizing-only, no code change** (0% parity, 2293-line diff, `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh --verbose polymorphism`). Live sizing plus a dedicated Explore-agent survey confirmed this is a genuine multi-file subsystem gap, not a single-loop-scoped narrow fix — five independent gaps, all real PG behavior goopg does not implement: (1) polymorphic-argument-resolution for `anyelement`/`anyarray`/`anynonarray`/`anyenum`/`anyrange`/`anymultirange`/the `anycompatible*` family is essentially unimplemented for SQL/plpgsql function calls — `pg_proc` stores the declared pseudo-type strings verbatim, `executeSQLRoutine` coerces args to the literal declared name, and the one real resolver (`resolvePolymorphicReturnType`, `internal/executor/plpgsql_runtime.go`) handles only 2 of 11 pseudo-types and only fires on an error-message branch, not real calls; (2) no SQL-function-body inlining (goopg's CONTEXT always reads "statement 1", never PG's "during inlining"); (3) range/multirange constructor functions (`int4range(...)`/`numrange(...)`/`multirange(...)` as callable functions, not just types) are entirely unimplemented — no Datum range-value model exists; (4) `CREATE FUNCTION`-time polymorphic-return-type validation is missing; (5) `pg_statistic` base table is not registered as queryable even though a real per-database heap already exists (only the derived `pg_stats` view is registered) — extending the existing pg_type/pg_attribute startup-registration pattern turned out to need per-connection dynamic scoping (pg_statistic's heap is written lazily per-DB by ANALYZE, unlike pg_type/pg_attribute's initdb-seeded single DefaultDBOid heap), not a copy-paste, so it was not landed half-correct under time pressure. Full itemized findings + file:line citations + resume points: `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md` 2026-08-25 M0134-0152. CSV row flipped `not-tried` → `failed`, `pass_required` stays `no`. Recommend polymorphism.sql become its own dedicated milestone ("polymorphic function type resolution") — consistent with how M0134 has already spun off GiST/SPGiST plan integration, pg_shdepend/CASCADE, and the Large Object facility as dedicated-milestone candidates. - [x] **M0134-0153 — float4.sql** — regress-sql `failed` → **PARKED 2026-08-25, real fix shipped** (6 gaps ledgered, file stays `failed`): landed `float4send`/`float8send` as callable scalar functions (`internal/executor/expr.go` evalFuncCall — pg_proc already seeded both OIDs 2425/2427 but no dispatch case existed for any `*send` function family, so every call 42883'd; new cases reuse the existing binary-encode logic from `copy_binary.go`'s FORMAT-binary COPY/wire arm, `pgFloatFromDatum` + `math.FloatNbits` big-endian pack). This cleared ~500 diff lines (every `float4send(...)` bit-pattern round-trip assertion in the file). Remaining diff, still real PG behavior goopg lacks (full detail + file:line citations in `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md` 2026-08-25 M0134-0153): (1) float4in error-message/SQLSTATE parity — overflow inputs like `'10e70'::float4` must report 22003 `"10e70" is out of range for type real` with a LINE echo, goopg reports 22P02 `invalid input syntax for type real: "10e70"` with no LINE (ties into standing gap #19, `coerceTextLikeDatum` never threads `ExecError.Pos`); leading/trailing whitespace is stripped before quoting in the error text, PG preserves it verbatim (`"  "` should echo as `"  "` not `""`); malformed NaN/Infinity variants (`'N A N'`, `'NaN x'`) are silently accepted instead of erroring. (2) `'nan'::float4 / '0'::float4` raises division-by-zero; PG's float division defines nan/0 = NaN (`postgres/src/backend/utils/adt/float.c` `float4_div` only raises division-by-zero for a literal-zero *dividend* check that excludes NaN numerator). (3) unary `@` (absolute value) prefix operator on float4 is a parser gap — `SELECT @f.f1` 42601s ("expected expression (got @)"); the binary `@` circle/box containment operators exist but the unary float abs-value operator does not. (4) `'9223372036854775807'::float4::int8` should raise 22003 `bigint out of range` (float4→int8 boundary rounding); goopg silently wraps to `-9223372036854775808`. (5) `CREATE CAST (xfloat4 AS float4) WITHOUT FUNCTION` false-positives "source data type and target data type are the same" for a `CREATE TYPE ... (like = float4)` shell-domain type distinct from `float4` — the cast-validation same-type check is comparing by underlying storage representation instead of type OID/name. (6) `bits::integer` where `bits` is a `bit(32)` literal (`x'00000001'`) goes through a string-of-'0'/'1'-digits→decimal-integer path (`parseIntegerInput`) instead of reinterpreting the bit pattern as a two's-complement int4 (PG's `bittoint4`) — no bit(n)→integer cast dispatch exists at all. Each of (1)-(6) is independently scoped; none blocks the others, so any can be picked up next without redoing this loop's float4send work. Gates run: `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh --verbose float4` (0% → still 0% file-level pass but diff shrank materially), `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2/Q13=35), `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS. CSV rationale updated (still `failed`/`pass_required=no`) via `make regen-testport`.
- [x] **M0134-0154 — privileges.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **PARKED 2026-08-25, sizing-only, no code change** (0/1 PASS, 0% parity, 3926-line diff — the largest M0134 case sized to date; `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh --verbose privileges`). A scoped fix was attempted first: `has_table_privilege` alone is the single largest contributor (64 of the diff's `does not exist` occurrences; `pg_proc` already carries every `has_table_privilege` OID but `evalFuncCall` has zero dispatch `case` for it or any of its 13 sibling `has_*_privilege`/`pg_has_role` functions — the same float4send/float8send-shaped gap M0134-0153 fixed, but ~14x the function-family breadth). Investigating it live surfaced a deeper blocker: goopg's existing ACL-check core (`dmlPrivilegePermittedAs`/`catalog.InMemory.HasTablePrivilege`, `internal/executor/operators_storage.go:2233`, `internal/catalog/catalog.go:16785`) checks ONLY the exact session role string — no PUBLIC-pseudo-role fallback (`GRANT ... TO PUBLIC` is stored under literal key `"public"` but never consulted for other roles) and no role-inheritance-chain walk (PG's `aclmask`/`roles_is_member_of` in `acl.c` ORs together the querying role, every role it transitively INHERITs, and PUBLIC). A faithful `has_table_privilege` needs that core fixed first, but the core is shared by every DML privilege check project-wide (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/SELECT gating), making it a correctness-sensitive shared-core change, not a same-loop narrow fix — landing `has_table_privilege` on top of the broken core risked silently missing PUBLIC/inherited grants on a security-relevant function. Four further independent gap classes surfaced and ledgered without code changes: `REASSIGN OWNED BY`/`DROP OWNED BY` are entirely unparsed (syntax errors); role-membership CASCADE-drop dependency tracking is absent (`DROP ROLE` never checks `pg_auth_members.grantor` references, ties into the standing pg_shdepend/CASCADE milestone candidate already noted in M0134-0152); the Large Object facility (`lo_create`/`lo_import`/`lo_export`/`lo_unlink`, `pg_largeobject`) is unimplemented (already a standing milestone candidate, reconfirmed here, 17 diff occurrences); and several narrower items (`pg_get_acl`/`makeaclitem` undispatched, inconsistent `MAINTAIN`-privilege enforcement for `CLUSTER`/`REINDEX`/`REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`, missing `information_schema.table_privileges` and `pg_shmem_allocations*` views). - [x] **M0134-0155 — psql.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **sized live + scoped fixes landed 2026-08-25** (still failing: 3385-line diff; `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh --verbose psql`). Landed: (1) `SET ROLE TO/= x` generic_set spellings end-to-end — parser role arm in `parseSet` (`=` must be matched as TokenOperator, not acceptSymbol — the lexer emits `=` as an operator, which silently broke the first cut until the new parser tests caught it) and the query.go/extended.go wire fast paths via `stripSetToOrEquals`; upstream psql.sql uses `SET ROLE TO regress_display_role` (:545), and PG 18.3 accepts the spellings because unreserved-keyword ROLE also parses through generic_set (gram.y:1656-1693 + :1754 dedicated bare form; oracle-verified). (2) The sibling SESSION AUTHORIZATION fast paths deliberately do NOT take TO/= (dedicated productions gram.y:1764/:1774 only — oracle-verified 42601): a `setAuthzGenericSetForm` guard now routes those spellings from both protocols' fast paths to the parser-driven path for the true error instead of applying literal garbage role "TO x". (3) Shared-core hole exposed by oracle-diffing that route and fixed here: PARSE-time errors inside an explicit transaction now abort the block (connTx.Fail + ReleasePinnedSnapshotOnFail at dispatch.go's parse-error return and dispatch_extended.go's qerr return, mirroring M0132-S5's plan-error gate); empty statements remain allowed in an aborted block (`allowedInAbortedBlock`, upstream bug #17983 parity). (4) `CREATE TABLE (...) USING <access_method>` accepted-and-discarded on both parseCreateTableTail arms incl. the empty-column-list suffix arm. Tests: parser SET subtests, TestParseCreateTableUsingAccessMethod, TestStripSetToOrEquals, TestFastPathGenericSetSpellings, TestParseErrorAbortsTransactionBlock (both protocols + #17983 rule). Harness fixes: pg-oracle-diff.sh --auto-start passed nonexistent initdb `-q` and relied on the OS-user superuser default while connecting as postgres (removed / pinned `-U "$USER_"`). Residual (63 goopg-side ERRORs, ≥6 subsystems): role-does-not-exist ×14, syntax-error ×11 (incl. unknown-statement template divergence "unsupported statement (got X)" vs upstream "syntax error at or near"), spurious NOT NULL ×6, permission-denied-to-create-role ×5, function-does-not-exist ×5, table-permission denials ×9 (M0134-0154 ACL core), pg_roles.rolinherit ×2; one-character oracle residue: error text echoes lowercased keyword ('to') where scan.c echoes raw source ('TO') — cross-cutting, ledgered. Full itemization: `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md` 2026-08-25 M0134-0155; CSV row flipped `not-tried` → `failed` via `make regen-testport` / `make check-testport-inventory` (both PASS). - [x] **M0134-0156 — psql_crosstab.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **PASS 2026-08-29, real fix shipped** (0% → **100% parity**, 65-line diff → 0; CSV flipped `not-tried` → `pass` / `pass_required=yes`). Despite the name, nothing was wrong with psql's client-side `\crosstabview` — every diverging line came from two planner bugs its fixture reaches. (1) **VALUES column type resolution ignored PG's `select_common_type`** (`postgres/src/backend/parser/parse_coerce.c:1342`, via `transformValuesClause` `parse_clause.c:1675`), whose defining property is that UNKNOWNOID literals are *skipped*, so any row supplying a real type wins regardless of position, with an all-unknown → TEXT fallback (`parse_coerce.c:1451`). goopg had two both-wrong sibling implementations: `planStandaloneValuesSelect` unified across rows but `exprType(*StringConst)` is `"text"` and `unifyValueTypes` treats text as the sink, so `date` + bare literals collapsed to `text`; `planValuesSubquery` used the **first row's type only**, with no unification at all. They masked each other (the subquery form happened to be right for a typed-first fixture, wrong if the rows were reversed). Damage was worse than a wrong `pg_typeof`: with the column typed `text`, `CREATE TABLE ctv_data (…) AS VALUES` stringified the already-typed date **through DateStyle** into `04-01-2015`, and `EXTRACT(year FROM d)` then hard-errored 42883 (`internal/executor/expr.go:7343`) because `parseCopyTimestamp` cannot read it. Landed shared `valuesCandidateType` + `resolveValuesColumnType` (`internal/optimizer/planner.go`) called by BOTH planners; `exprType` deliberately unchanged (the unknown-literal notion exists only inside common-type resolution). (2) **`groupExprName` was a private mini-copy of `FigureColname`** (`parse_target.c`) knowing only ColumnRef and FuncCall, so a `GROUP BY` degraded an `EXTRACT` target label to `?column?` — PG has exactly one such routine and grouping does not change what it returns; it now delegates to `targetMeta`. Design: `docs/design/m0134-0156-values-common-type.md`, indexed in `docs/design/README.md`. Regression tests: `internal/optimizer/values_common_type_test.go` (`TestValuesColumnTypeSelectCommonType` — typed row first *and* last × standalone/subquery, all-unknown → text, NULL literal; `TestGroupByTargetLabelMatchesUngrouped` — asserts grouped == ungrouped label, the actual PG invariant). Ledger row `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md` 2026-08-29 M0134-0156 (goopg types EVERY integer literal int8 where PG uses int4 when it fits; common-type resolution now propagates that divergence into VALUES). Gates: `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh --verbose psql_crosstab` **100% PASS**; 20-file before/after regress sweep (select aggregates groupingsets union case insert subselect join with limit select_having select_implicit select_distinct int4 int8 numeric date timestamp text arrays) — **no regressions**, `numeric` 3830→3828 and `join` 20939→20924 both IMPROVED (correct psql right-alignment of properly typed columns; a duplicated `Join Filter` restating its own `Hash Cond` removed, `count(*)` unchanged); `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS (exit 0); `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2 rows 20.07s, Q13=34 rows 7.77s); `make regen-testport` / `make check-testport-inventory` PASS. **OUTSTANDING: the TPC-DS SF0.5 gate was NOT run** — it hard-refuses while the nightly CI batch is live, and forcing it would have corrupted the nightly's pgbench TPS stage into false action items; next loop should run `FORCE=1 GOOPG_BIN=$PWD/tmp/goopg-sf05-bin scripts/tpcds-sf05-regression.sh sweep` once run 20260828-235424 is done. - [x] **M0134-0157 — psql_pipeline.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **RUN for the first time and PARKED 2026-08-29**, contained fix shipped (CSV row flipped `not-tried` → `failed`, `pass_required` stays `no`). Sized at HEAD: 357 diff lines / 22 `^+ERROR` / 12 `^-ERROR`, and — unusually for an M0134 case — only **two** root causes, one of which cascades over ~85% of the file. **Dominant, REFACTOR-tier: goopg's extended protocol has no implicit transaction block.** `executeExtendedQueryViaExecutor` (`internal/postmaster/dispatch_extended.go:171-201`) is auto-commit-per-Execute and `MsgSync` (`internal/postmaster/server.go:1820`) only clears `syncRequired` + writes ReadyForQuery, so nothing represents `TBLOCK_IMPLICIT_INPROGRESS`. PG never commits at Execute: it sets `XACT_FLAGS_PIPELINING` (`postgres/src/backend/tcop/postgres.c:2317`), flips `TBLOCK_STARTED` → `TBLOCK_IMPLICIT_INPROGRESS` on the NEXT `start_xact_command` (`BeginImplicitTransactionBlock`, `access/transam/xact.c:4326`), and commits only at Sync after `EndImplicitTransactionBlock` (`postgres.c:4968`). Consequences the case asserts and goopg gets wrong: the FIRST command of a message group is not in a transaction block while every later one is (`REINDEX CONCURRENTLY`/`VACUUM` succeed first and fail second; `LOCK`/`SET LOCAL` the reverse), and a mid-group `BEGIN` converts the implicit block IN PLACE so a later `ROLLBACK` undoes statements sent *before* it. goopg instead commits the pre-`BEGIN` INSERT, so the next pipeline hits `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "psql_pipeline_pkey"` inside an explicit block whose `COMMIT` is (correctly) skipped-until-Sync — leaving the session aborted for the remaining ~300 lines. goopg already has the SIMPLE-path analogue (one transaction per Query message + mid-batch `BEGIN` promotion, `internal/postmaster/dispatch.go:1071`, guarded by `TestSimpleQueryBatchAbortUndoesEarlierCreateTable`), and the design doc carries a 5-step port. **Not attempted this loop**: it is a transaction-lifecycle change touching every extended-protocol client, and the TPC-DS SF0.5 gate hard-refuses while the nightly CI batch is live (run `20260828-235424` executed for this entire loop), so it could not have been verified before commit. **Shipped the CONTAINED cause**: Bind/Describe protocol-error TEXT parity in `internal/postmaster/extended.go` — `bind message supplies %d parameters, but prepared statement "%s" requires %d` (`postgres.c:1729`), `bind message has %d parameter formats but %d parameters` (`:1723`), and the semantically-distinct unnamed-statement case, where PG consults the prepared-statement table only for a non-empty name and reports `unnamed prepared statement does not exist` with no name at all (`:1671` Bind, `:2669` Describe `S`); the two sibling lookups now share one `missingPreparedStatement` helper. SQLSTATEs and the parameter-format predicate were already correct and are unchanged. **357 → 291 diff lines (-66)**, `^+ERROR` 22 → 15. Design: `docs/design/m0134-0157-extended-implicit-transaction-block.md`, indexed in `docs/design/README.md`. Guard: `internal/postmaster/extended_bind_message_parity_test.go` (`TestExtendedBindMessageTextMatchesUpstream`, 8 subtests). Ledger rows `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md` 2026-08-29 M0134-0157 (×2 — the implicit-block feature, and a by-catch: `handleBindFrame` rejects any Bind with >1 **result** format code where PG accepts one per output column, `pquery.c:642`). Next M0134 task to select: **M0134-0158**.
- [x] **M0134-0158 — publication.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **sized live for the first time and PARKED 2026-08-29**, two cross-cutting parser gaps FIXED (CSV row flipped `not-tried` → `failed`, `pass_required` stays `no`; 2360 → **2233** diff lines, `^+ERROR` 171 → **111**). The file's single largest contributor turned out not to be a publication feature at all: **69 of the 171 goopg-side errors (40%) were the identical `syntax error at or near "OPERATOR"`, on SQL that psql GENERATES** — `processSQLNamePattern` (`postgres/src/fe_utils/string_utils.c:1121-1152`) appends `<namevar> OPERATOR(pg_catalog.~) '^(pat)$' COLLATE pg_catalog.default` to **every** describe meta-command given a pattern, so the gap was every `\d name` / `\dt pat` / `\di pat` / `\dv pat` in the WHOLE regress corpus, not `\dRp+`. It needed two independent fixes and neither alone changes anything observable. **(A)** Upstream factors the operator position of an expression through `qual_Op` (`gram.y:16658`, `Op | OPERATOR '(' any_operator ')'`), used in exactly four rules — `a_expr qual_Op a_expr` (`:15009`), `qual_Op a_expr` (`:15011`) and the `b_expr` pair (`:15488`, `:15490`) — and goopg had only the bare-`Op` half of all four. Ported as a NEW `qual_op` nonterminal carrying only the parenthesised half, so the four proven bare-`Op` rules are untouched (`+ - = < >` arrive as char terminals with alternatives of their own, which upstream reaches via `MathOp`/`all_Op`); `%prec Op` is mandatory because the bodies end in a nonterminal. It reuses `any_operator_name`/`op_run` (`grammar/goopg_ext.y:3202`) to rejoin the per-character `Op` tokens the lexer splits, which stays unambiguous because the run is bounded by the `')'`. The QUALIFIER is dropped, not resolved — goopg's AST has no schema-qualified operator node, every operator is an `OpCode`, so the qualified spelling folds to byte-identical AST (`TestQualifiedOperatorFoldsLikeBareSpelling` asserts the property directly rather than one golden at a time), where upstream resolves in the named schema (`LookupOperName`, `parse_oper.c:99`) — ledgered. `qualPrefixExpr` routes `OPERATOR(pg_catalog.-) 1` through the same `doNegate` fold `-1` uses. **(B)** Exposed one token to the right once (A) landed: `COLLATE` took `ColId '.' ColId` where upstream takes `any_name` (`gram.y:14867`) whose second component is `attr_name: ColLabel` (`:9161`, `:17724`) — the all-keywords label — so `pg_catalog.default`, the only qualified collation psql ever writes and the one whose tail is a RESERVED keyword, was unreachable. Now `ColId '.' as_col_label`. Hard-won Rule #6 followed: playbook §12 re-read before touching `grammar/*.y`, built with `make gen-parser`, and the golden diff is the review artifact — **conflict pin held at exactly 59** on the unchanged token allowlist, and golden regeneration was **22 insertions / 1 deletion** where the deletion is the statement-count header (no existing pin moved, i.e. the change is purely additive). Design: `docs/design/m0134-0158-qualified-operator-and-collate-any-name.md`, indexed in `docs/design/README.md`. Guards: `internal/parser/qual_op_test.go` (`TestQualifiedOperatorParity`, `TestQualifiedOperatorFoldsLikeBareSpelling`, `TestCollateAnyNameParity`, `TestPatternOperatorSpellingsStillUnsupported`). Gates: `go test ./internal/parser/` PASS; `make gen-parser` conflict gate PASS; **13-file before/after regress sweep** (HEAD worktree baseline vs working tree) — net **−1848 diff lines, ZERO regressions**: `constraints` 745→**167**, `alter_table` 4247→**3776**, `inherit` 3689→**3250**, `create_index` 3459→**3340**, `insert` 1212→**1161**, `identity` 1061→**1035**, `collate` 555→**536**, `matview` 378→**369**, `domain` 1759→**1751**, `numeric` 3828→**3825**, `publication` 2360→**2233**; `create_view` (+7) and `dependency` (+2) grew only because a query that used to die as a 3-line syntax error now EXECUTES and prints a longer still-diverging table, and across all 13 files exactly one new `+ERROR` class appears (`pg_catalog.pg_relation_is_publishable does not exist`, reachable only because `\dRp+` now parses). `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units` PASS; `make regen-testport` / `make check-testport-inventory` PASS. Ledger rows `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md` 2026-08-29 M0134-0158 (×4: qualifier ignored not resolved; `qual_all_Op`/`subquery_Op` not ported; the `~~`/`!~~` family, whose BARE path MISPARSES as `~ (~ x)` because the lexer splits the run; and the remaining publication grammar subset). Next M0134 task to select: **M0134-0159**.
- [x] **M0134-0162 — roleattributes.sql** — CLOSED 2026-08-29 (`not-tried` → **`pass`**, 28 → **0** diff lines). The case's entire divergence was one root cause: `[NO]INHERIT` was accept-and-ignore engine-wide — `catalog.RoleAttrs` had no `Inherit` field, `applyRoleAttrOptions` never probed for it, and both `pg_authid` row builders hardcoded `rolinherit = 't'`. The non-cosmetic half is that `rolinherit` is PG's DEFAULT for `pg_auth_members.inherit_option` (`user.c:1924-1939`) and goopg's `HasPrivsOfRole`/`SelectBestAdmin` traverse only inherit-marked rows, so the column and the membership default are one slice — landing the first without the second would have left a NOINHERIT role inheriting every privilege of every role granted to it. Design `docs/design/m0134-0162-rolinherit-attribute.md`; 8-case regress A/B vs a HEAD worktree, zero regressions; CSV flipped to `pass`/`pass_required=yes`.
- [x] **M0134-0165 — security_label.sql** — **CLOSED GREEN 2026-08-29** (regress-sql `not-tried` → **`pass`**, 16 → **0** diff lines, byte-identical). The second full close in the run sequence, not a park. The case is eight `SECURITY LABEL` statements that must all fail, and goopg already matched the interesting half byte-for-byte — including PG's ordering quirk that `ExecSecLabelStmt` raises "no security label providers have been loaded" BEFORE resolving the target, so a nonexistent table still reports the provider error rather than 42P01. The whole 16-line diff was the two-line scaffold: `SET client_min_messages TO 'warning'` then `DROP ROLE IF EXISTS`, whose "does not exist, skipping" NOTICEs goopg printed anyway. Root cause **engine-wide and total**: `client_min_messages` existed only as a GUC *declaration* (`internal/utils/misc/defaults.go`) — a whole-tree grep found exactly ONE reference, the declaration itself — so **nothing consumed it**; every NOTICE/WARNING ever produced went to the client unconditionally and the `SET` succeeded while doing nothing. Fixed where upstream puts it, at the single emitter rather than the producers: `ShouldOutputToClient` (`internal/libpq/messages.go`) reproduces `should_output_to_client` (`postgres/src/backend/utils/error/elog.c`, `elevel >= client_min_messages || elevel == INFO`) over upstream's `elog.h` elevels, and `WriteNoticeResponse` consults it through a new per-connection `FrameWriter.ClientMinMessagesFn` hook (the existing `TxStatusFn` precedent) installed in `runPostStartupLoop`. Two details a naive `>=` gets wrong are pinned: INFO is exempt ("always sent regardless of client_min_messages", elog.h), and ERROR/FATAL/PANIC (21/22/23) clear the GUC's `error` ceiling (21) unconditionally — hence `WriteErrorResponse` carries NO gate. Choking at one function rather than the eleven `WriteNoticeResponse` call sites (dispatch.go ×5 incl. the isolation runner's inline `NoticeFlush`, extended.go ×2, database_ddl.go, copy.go) is deliberate and is upstream's own reason for putting the test inside `elog.c` — the twelfth producer cannot bypass the GUC; a `MsgNoticeResponse` grep confirms the single emission site. GUC read live per message so `SET`/`SET LOCAL`/`RESET` apply immediately; every unclassifiable input fails **open** (a swallowed warning is worse than a spurious one), which also reproduces upstream's `ClientAuthInProgress` carve-out for free since the hook is installed only after the handshake. Design `docs/design/m0134-0165-client-min-messages-notice-filter.md`. Gates: 16-case regress A/B vs a HEAD worktree — `security_label` 16 → **0 (PASS)**, `privileges` −28, `rowsecurity` −26, `foreign_data` −17, `alter_generic` −16, `alter_table` −12, `object_address` −12, eight cases byte-identical: **net −127 lines, ZERO regressions**. `plpgsql` 4399 → 4400 was adjudicated and DISMISSED — an A/A on the unchanged tree gave 4402 then 4401 with the same statement (`select * from f1(42)`) flipping among three outcomes, so the build disagrees with itself and the delta carries no signal (0165b). New guard `internal/libpq/client_min_messages_filter_test.go`, revert-checked. `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units` PASS; pre-commit pgbench smoke PASS.
- [x] **M0134-0166 — float8.sql** — **PARKED 2026-08-29** on the standing pattern (sized live for the first time: 1311 diff lines / 56 `^+ERROR` / 33 `^-ERROR` at HEAD; CSV row stays `failed`), and the loop again shipped an **engine-wide** fix the case merely exposed. The opening "underflow and overflow handling" block did not error — it *succeeded*: `'10e400'::float8` returned the string `10e400`, `'N A N'::float8` returned `N A N`, `'  NAN  '::float8` printed `NAN`. Root cause: `evalCast` (`internal/executor/expr.go`) had **no `KindString` arm at all** for `float4`/`real`/`float`/`float8`/`double precision`, so a text datum fell through to the arm's closing `return d, nil` and came back *unchanged and unvalidated* — every `'…'::float8` anywhere in goopg accepted arbitrary text into a float column. Same shape as M0134-0087 (`'…'::xid`) and M0134-0098 (`circle` as raw varlena). Float text input existed **four** times in four different states (cast / `float8 '…'` typed literal / `pgFloatFromDatum` column assignment / `pg_input_error_info`) — a textbook Hard-won Rule #2 cluster — and all four now share one `floatIn` (`internal/executor/float_in.go`) ported from `float8in_internal` (`postgres/src/backend/utils/adt/float.c:395`). Upstream's ORDER is load-bearing: the range check precedes the trailing-junk check (`'10e400junk'` reports the overflow), a 22003 names only the numeric **token** while a 22P02 names the **original untrimmed** string (`'  - 3'` → `… : "  - 3"`, which the old `TrimSpace`-first `pgFloatFromDatum` reported as `"- 3"`), and denormals are explicitly not errors. One place Go is not glibc: `strconv.ParseFloat` sets `ErrRange` only on OVERFLOW, so an underflow to zero returned `0, nil` and `'10e-400'::float8` would have been `0` instead of an error — `floatTokenIsZero` reconstructs the distinction while still accepting genuine zeros (`0`, `-0.0000`, `0e500`) and denormals (`5e-324`). Design `docs/design/m0134-0166-float8in-shared-input.md`. Gates: 20-case regress A/B vs a HEAD worktree — `float4` 844 → **652 (−192)**, `float8` 1311 → **1204 (−107)**, the other **18 byte-identical**: net **−299 lines, ZERO regressions**. New guard `internal/executor/float_in_test.go` (revert-checked). Remaining buckets filed as 0166a–0166d below.
- [x] **M0134-0167 — spgist.sql** — **PARKED 2026-08-29** on the standing pattern (sized live for the first time: `not-tried` → `failed`, **23 diff lines, ONE hunk**; CSV row stays `failed`), and the loop again shipped an **engine-wide** fix the case merely exposed. The case's entire divergence is that goopg has **no SP-GiST access method at all** — `USING spgist` registers catalog metadata only (`execCreateIndex`'s catalog-only branch, shared with gist/gin/brin), so `explain (costs off) select * from spgist_domain_tbl where f1 = 'fo'` plans a Seq Scan where PG plans `Bitmap Index Scan on spgist_domain_idx`. REFACTOR-tier, own milestone; everything else in the file already matched byte-for-byte, including the out-of-range `fillfactor` rejections M0134-0160 unlocked. Shipped instead, on the case's own `create index … using spgist(…)` theme: `catalog.IndexAMCapability` (M0134-0090) is a hand-curated 1:1 mirror of the six in-tree AMs' `IndexAmRoutine` flags whose doc comment says it is used *exclusively* by `pg_indexam_has_property` — and that was literally true, so the compat surface could **report** that spgist cannot do unique indexes while `execCreateIndex` happily created one. Of upstream's five `DefineIndex` capability checks (`indexcmds.c:869/874/879`, `:2228/2233`) goopg enforced ONE, and not by reading a flag: a hardcoded `case "brin", "gin", "hash":` INCLUDE list. `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX … USING spgist|gist|gin|brin|hash`, multicolumn spgist/hash, and `DESC`/`NULLS FIRST` on every orderless AM were all silently ACCEPTED — not cosmetic, since those four AMs are catalog-only in goopg, so the index advertised `indisunique` and enforced nothing. New `checkIndexAMCapabilities` (`internal/executor/amutils.go`) gives the table its second consumer, in upstream's order, on the **declared** AM name (before the `hash` → `btree` rewrite); method resolution + the gate also moved ahead of the reloption checks to match `DefineIndex`'s own order. Design `docs/design/m0134-0167-index-am-capability-gate.md`. 15-statement oracle A/B **byte-identical**; 15-case regress A/B vs a HEAD worktree **zero regressions** and no line movement, because **no upstream regress case exercises any of the five errors** — hence the revert-checked guard `internal/executor/index_am_capability_test.go` (12 subtests fail when reverted). Remaining buckets filed 0167a–0167d below.
- [x] **M0134-0175a — table `fillfactor` is never applied when choosing a heap insert page** — **DONE 2026-08-29** (`tablesample.sql` 304 → **214** diff lines; the first 63 lines of the case — every sampled `SELECT` — now match the oracle byte-for-byte). `fillfactor` existed everywhere except the one place it means something: parsed, bounds-checked into [10,100], persisted to `pg_class.reloptions`, pg_dump'd, ALTER-able, and read by the **cost model** — but with **no consumer in the insert path**, which moved to a new page only when the current one was *physically* full. The `WITH (fillfactor=10)` fixture, which upstream created for the express purpose of spanning pages from little data, packed into **1** block where PG 18.3 uses **4**. Landed: `storage.PageGetHeapFreeSpace` (`bufpage.c:988`) and `storage.HeapInsertTargetFreeSpace` port `RelationGetBufferForTuple`'s `targetFreeSpace` (`hio.c:520-556`), with `MaxHeapTupleSize`/`nearlyEmptyFreeSpace` joining the existing `MaxHeapTuplesPerPage`; `writeHeapRowReturning` computes the target once and gates the FSM-candidate and tail-block probes on it. **Three upstream properties are load-bearing, each mirrored:** the **default reserves nothing** (`fillfactor=100` ⇒ target = `MAXALIGN(len)`, arithmetically the old test — so every table without the reloption keeps its exact previous on-disk density, pinned by the `TestDefaultFillfactorPacksTightly` control); a **freshly extended page is exempt** (hio.c:859); and the **`nearlyEmptyFreeSpace` clamp**, without which a 4 KiB tuple in a `fillfactor=10` table demands ~11 KiB on an 8 KiB page and the relation extends forever (revert-checked, bites). A page rejected by the *reserve* records its **actual** free space in the FSM, not 0 — it is not full, and the search already asks for `targetFreeSpace`. **Sibling `writeHeapRowReturningPG` checked and deliberately NOT changed** (its only caller is `writeHeapRowCanonical` ⇒ catalog relations ⇒ fillfactor always 100 ⇒ unreachable code), with the reason and the port instruction left in a comment at the function. **Why a memo:** `catalog.InMemory` has no OID index — `LookupTableByOIDAllDBs` walks every namespace's `map[string]*Table` — so resolving per inserted row would put an O(tables) scan under a shared RLock on the hottest write path in the engine (the M0107 `ReadMemStats`-per-query shape). `(*Context).heapFillfactor` is the relcache stand-in: one resolution per relation per session, invalidated by ALTER TABLE SET/RESET; cross-session staleness is bounded to page density and is ledgered. Guards `TestFillfactorReservesSpaceAtInsert` (pins PG's 3/3/3/1 four-block layout), `TestDefaultFillfactorPacksTightly`, `TestHeapFillfactorMemoTracksAlter`, `TestHeapInsertTargetFreeSpace`, `TestPageGetHeapFreeSpaceTracksAdds`, `TestHeapPageSizeConstantsMatchPG`. Design `docs/design/m0134-0175a-fillfactor-at-insert.md`. Gates: units precommit PASS, `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12 rows=2, Q13 rows=34). Ledger: 3 rows 2026-08-29 (cross-session memo staleness; HOT-update reserve still unaudited; the new M0134-0175e cursor bug).
- [x] **M0134-0177 — test_setup.sql** — regress-sql `not-tried` → **`pass`** (`pass_required=yes`), **DONE 2026-08-29**: 237 lines, **100% parity**, zero divergence. Design `docs/design/m0134-0177-btree-split-posting-refill.md`; guard `internal/access/nbtree/split_posting_refill_test.go` (revert-checked). The case already matched PG 18.3 byte-for-byte — it was `not-tried` because it could not be **RUN**: `pg-regress-runner.sh` excludes `test_setup.sql` from `--all` discovery (correctly — it is the prerequisite, not a case), and naming it explicitly executed it **twice**, once as its own setup and again as the test. The second pass `COPY`d into an already-populated `onek`/`tenk1` and **crashed the backend**: `panic="storage: not enough free space in page"` from `nbtree.mustInsertItemSorted` under `CopyFromExecutor.insertSourceRow`. **The shipped fix is engine-wide, not test_setup-specific**, and any duplicate-heavy index reaches it: `pageItems` **expands** posting lists into one item per heap TID, but the split refill wrote every expanded entry back as its own plain line pointer — a posting pays the key overhead once per RUN, the expanded form once per TID. Instrumenting the crashing split measured it: a leaf whose data budget is **8132** bytes expanded to **21960**, so both halves of a 50/50 cut overflowed (normal splits log ~8700 = "one page plus the new item", the invariant the path silently assumes). `byteAwareSplitLoc` compounded it by pricing an item as `SizeOfIndexTupleData + len(key)` — no line pointer, no MAXALIGN, no posting awareness — a *different* expression from `itemEncodedSize` (the root-0040 shape one layer up). Upstream never hits this: `_bt_split` partitions **raw on-page items** and only touches the posting the new item lands inside, via `_bt_swap_posting`, which preserves byte length. Fix keeps the expanded working form and **re-forms postings on the way out**: `postingChunkLens` is now the single statement of the chunking rule that `deduplicateToRawItems` WRITES from and `runFootprint` BUDGETS from (anti-drift equality pinned by a test); `refillDeduplicated` replaces the per-TID refill on all three whole-page rewrites — landing the M0055-0003 "Phase B" that `dedupConsolidate`'s own comment had deferred; `compactSplitLoc` balances on the compact footprint and **refuses** when no cut fits, so the caller errors with the latch coherent instead of resetting the page and panicking mid-refill. **The revert-check is the sharpest finding**: at HEAD the guard fails `RangeScan returned 21396 entries, want 21600` — the old split path did not only crash on some shapes, it **silently dropped 204 index entries** on others. `byteAwareSplitLoc`/`compactRawSize` deleted rather than left as unreachable siblings stating the wrong size model. Harness half: the runner now skips ALL setup when `test_setup` is the named case (the four prerequisites read tables it creates). Deferred and ledgered: the split still round-trips expanded→compact instead of partitioning raw items (so these splits fall back to a full-page-image WAL record), and `insertItemSorted`'s binary search still lacks the heap-TID tiebreak `appendSorted` gained. Gates: nbtree+storage PASS, units PASS, tpch-spotcheck PASS (Q12 rows=2, Q13 rows=34), regress quick set A/B vs stashed HEAD **byte-identical**.

- [x] **M0134-0185 — vacuum_parallel.sql** — CLOSED 2026-09-01. Sized live (0/1 PASS, 16 diff lines): sole break was `VACUUM (PARALLEL 4, INDEX_CLEANUP ON) t` — `syntax error at or near "ON"`. Added `ON { $$ = "on" }`; conflict pin unchanged at 60. Fixed for symmetry alongside: `vacuumNamedOpt`'s `index_cleanup` switch and `isFalseWord` (`internal/parser/support.go`) only matched literal `"false"`, so the already-parsing `OFF` spelling silently no-op'd (also affected `TRUNCATE OFF`/`PROCESS_MAIN OFF`/`PROCESS_TOAST OFF`).
