New test `TestParseVacuumIndexCleanupOnOff` (`internal/parser/parser_test.go`). 100% parity via `TestPort_RegressSuite/vacuum_parallel`. CSV flipped to `pass`/`pass_required=yes`. Design `docs/design/0100-0149/m0134-0185-vacuum-parallel-index-cleanup-on.md`.
## M0134 — Completed sub-task items (archived 2026-09-01)

Completed indented `[x]` sub-tasks moved out of `.ralph/fix_plan.md`.

  - [x] **M0134-0005ai — a statement shape that never parsed, and a blind fallback that reported success anyway** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **257 / 13** baseline left by 0005ah; exits at **230 lines / 12 hunks** (−27 lines, −1 hunk, no new `@@`, no existing hunk grew). The `constraint_comments` window showed four `COMMENT ON CONSTRAINT … ON DOMAIN …` statements where PG raises four distinct errors and goopg printed a bare `COMMENT` tag; "goopg's messages are wrong" is **wrong twice**. (1) The executor's `"constraint"` arm is never reached for that spelling — `parseCommentOnTail`'s CONSTRAINT branch (`internal/parser/parser.go:3118`) had no `ON DOMAIN` handling, consumed the bare ident `domain` as the object name and failed the shared `expected IS` trailer at `:3389`: the statement **never parsed**. (2) The parse failure was then converted into a success — `compatNoopCommandTag` (`internal/postmaster/dispatch.go:1752-1779`) prefix-matches raw `"comment on "` and writes `CommandComplete("COMMENT")` with zero catalog access (verified live: `… ON DOMAIN 12345 !!! IS 'x'` also returns `COMMENT`, `pg_description` unchanged). Corollary recorded in the design doc: the ONE line in this window that MATCHED PG was a coincidence of identical wire text over different mechanisms — a test pinning only that line would be a false-positive guard (2nd occurrence in this campaign, cf. §37). Fix: parser sets `cs.ObjKind = "domain constraint"` on an optional `domain` ident-keyword (mirrors the top-level COMMENT ON DOMAIN case at `:3256`); new executor `case "domain constraint"` resolves the **domain first, constraint second** — PG's order, not incidental (`objectaddress.c:975-990` resolves the type before `get_domain_constraint_oid`, so a missing domain reports `type … does not exist`), with PG's verbatim `constraint %q for domain %s does not exist` (`pg_constraint.c:1391`). **New machinery:** pre-slice `grep -rn "must be owner" internal/executor/*.go` returned ZERO hits — goopg's COMMENT ON had no ownership check for ANY object kind, where PG's `comment.c:CommentObject` calls `check_object_ownership` unconditionally. Two helpers (`checkCommentObjectOwnerOID` for a domain's typowner, `checkCommentObjectOwner` for `catalog.Table.Owner`'s role-NAME string) raise 42501 `must be owner of {relation,type} <name>` with superuser bypass, and the table-constraint arm was refactored to OID-first resolution so the check sits between address-resolution and the `pg_description` write — PG's ordering. Scoped to the two constraint arms only; every other COMMENT ON kind stays ungated — **ledgered**. The `compatNoopCommandTag` fallback was probed and deliberately RESTORED (`dispatch.go` has zero diff): removing it reddens `TestExtendedProtocolCompatNoopCommentOnMalformed`, which pins a DIFFERENT deliberate invariant (M0097-0023, silent absorption of a truncated clause of a *supported* kind). The fallback is load-bearing for two distinct reasons — that, plus `parseCommentOnTail`'s bare `default:` for genuinely unimplemented ObjKinds (AGGREGATE/LANGUAGE/ROLE/TABLESPACE/TEXT SEARCH …, none tested) — so its stale doc comment at `dispatch.go:1800-1804` is left intact and **ledgered** rather than half-corrected. Guards (`internal/executor/comment_on_domain_constraint_test.go`, FAIL-pre/PASS-post): domain-spelling success, unknown constraint, unknown domain, non-owner rejection on both spellings, owner + superuser acceptance. Design §40. Gates: `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/parser/... ./internal/executor/ ./internal/postmaster/` PASS, regress `constraints` 257/13 → 230/12, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS, pgbench smoke via the hook. Three deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19.
  - [x] **M0134-0005aj — `ORDER BY …::regclass` sorted by the rendered relation NAME, because the cast stringified two layers earlier** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **230 / 12** baseline left by 0005ai; exits at **216 lines / 11 hunks** (hunk 12 closed, no new `@@`, no existing hunk grew). PG has **no comparison operators for `regclass` at all** (zero hits for all eleven reg* types across `postgres/src/include/catalog/{pg_operator,pg_opclass,pg_amop}.dat`); `regclass→oid` is an IMPLICIT BINARY-COERCIBLE cast (`pg_cast.dat:182-185`), so `ORDER BY` resolves through to `oid`'s btree comparator `btoidcmp` (`nbtcompare.c:441`) — an **unsigned OID** compare, with the name merely *rendered*. goopg inverted that: `internal/executor/expr.go:806-845` (the `CastExpr` arm for `regclass` with a `KindInt` source) eagerly renders the relation name via `NewStringDatum(tbl.Name)` at eval time, so `sortOp.lessRows` reached `compareDatum` (`expr.go:2651`) holding two plain strings and did `strings.Compare`. **The ordering bug is a consequence of a representation bug**, and the representation bug is wider: the eager render fires only on the `KindInt` source arm, while a string-literal source (`'pg_class'::regclass`) and real regclass-typed columns (`pg_class.reltoastrelid`, `pg_partition_tree()`) stay `KindInt` end-to-end — two disagreeing representations of one type, the sibling-divergence class. Live oracle probes proved this already yields wrong ANSWERS, not just wrong order: `oid::regclass = 1259` → goopg `f` / PG `t`, and `oid::regclass = 'pg_class'::regclass` → goopg `f` / PG `t` (two casts of the SAME relation disagree). **The root-cause fix was scoped out deliberately and is ledgered as one indivisible slice**, because the obvious "just stop stringifying" one-liner is a trap: without a matching comparison-path fix it would redden `oid::regclass IN ('a_star','c_star')` (`src/test/regress/sql/create_misc.sql:250-251`), which passes today only *coincidentally* (goopg compares name-string to name-literals; PG resolves the IN-list via `select_common_type` + `regclassin`) — a strictly worse intermediate state, not a partial fix. Doing it properly needs a static type hint on the comparison IR (`optimizer.InExpr` + the bare-`=` node) since `Datum` carries a `Kind` and no type tag, making reg*-ness unrecoverable inside `compareEq`/`promoteCrossKind`: 150-250+ lines across three packages. What landed is Sort-operator-local (`internal/executor/operators.go`, 48 lines): `isRegSortFamilyTypeName` (single membership test for the eleven reg* types, carrying the oracle citations and an explicit do-not-reuse-for-`=`/`IN`/hashing warning) and `evalSortKeyValue` (for a reg*-family `*optimizer.CastExpr` key, evaluate `.Operand` — a `KindInt` result IS the OID; otherwise evaluate the full cast, whose `KindString` arm already resolves a name to a `KindInt` OID). **Both branches yield a `KindInt` OID, so the two source shapes now sort consistently with each other** — that consistency, not merely the row order, is the point. `sortOp.lessRows` calls it for both operands, preserving the `sortErr` strict-weak-ordering discipline; ASC/DESC/NULLS untouched. `ORDER BY <ordinal>` comes free and was **verified, not assumed**: `resolveOrderBySubstitution` (`internal/optimizer/planner.go:5511`) substitutes the ordinal with the target-list AST *before* lowering to `CastExpr`, so `ORDER BY 1` — the shape the real PG corpus uses at `src/test/regress/sql/partition_prune.sql:283` — reaches the helper with the real cast node. Guards (`internal/executor/operators_regsort_test.go`, FAIL-pre/PASS-post over a fixture whose OID order and NAME order genuinely DISAGREE — one where they agree proves nothing): ASC, DESC, mixed-key tiebreak, string-source vs int-source consistency. Design §41. Gates: `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/optimizer/ ./internal/postmaster/` PASS, regress `constraints` 230/12 → 216/11, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS, `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35 canonical — Hard-won Rule #1, executor change), pgbench smoke via the hook. Three deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19 (full Option B representation fix; unaudited hash-keying risk for GROUP BY/DISTINCT/hash-join; missing `22P02` parity on `x::regclass = '<literal>'`, a shape PG rejects via `oidin` not `regclassin`).
  - [x] **M0134-0005ak — validating a constraint validated only the table you named** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **216 / 11** baseline left by 0005aj; exits at **188 lines / 9 hunks** (census hunks 10 and 11 both closed, no new `@@`, no existing hunk grew). Symptom was a stale flag: after `ALTER TABLE notnull_inhparent ALTER i SET NOT NULL`, PG reports `convalidated=t` on `notnull_inhchild`/`notnull_inhgrand` and goopg reported `f`; same shape on the partitioned tree (`notnull_tbl1_3`'s `nn3`). **goopg's own in-code comment was the misdirection** — it cited `tablecmds.c:7950-8010` and claimed PG's `ATExecSetNotNull` existing-constraint branch "returns WITHOUT recursing to children", which is true of two of the three existing-tuple sub-arms and **false of the third, the one this test exercises**: `if (!conForm->convalidated)` flips nothing itself, it delegates entirely to `ATExecValidateConstraint` (`tablecmds.c:12908`). *Validation is a different operation from marking, and it is the one that recurses.* `ATExecValidateConstraint` dispatches (`:12974-12976`) to `QueueNNConstraintValidation` (`:13220-13309`), which at the top-level call walks **`find_all_inheritors`** (`:13245-13246`) — every descendant at every depth, partitions included, NOT `find_inheritance_children`'s single level — skipping the relation itself (`:13256-13257`) and any descendant already `convalidated` (`:13277-13278`), and treating a descendant with no matching constraint as an internal `elog` invariant violation (`:13272-13274`), not a user error. goopg had **two independent gaps, not one**: the `AlterTableSetNotNull` merge branch flipped only the parent's `NotValid` and left `shouldCascade` false (the existing `cascadeNotNullToChildrenAt` call belongs to the sibling "no constraint yet" arm), and the named `ALTER TABLE … VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` NOT NULL sub-branch had the identical defect separately — fixing either alone would have left a diverging path (the sibling-path rule, paid again). Fix (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`, 91 lines): new `cascadeNotNullValidateToDescendants` wired at BOTH call sites, deliberately a separate helper rather than a mode flag on `cascadeNotNullToChildrenAt` (whose merge sub-branch only bumps `InhCount` and never touches `NotValid` — ADD, VALIDATE and DROP are three different operations, and DROP is already factored out for the same reason). It mirrors the DROP twins' shape (`collectInheritanceAndPartitionChildren`, edge-keyed `visited`, `maxNotNullCascadeDepth`, `syncConstraintCatalogRow` per touched child) and **self-recurses**, because goopg has no one-shot `find_all_inheritors` — only single-level `InheritanceChildren`/`PartitionChildren` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:4489`, `:4510`). **One deliberate divergence, and it is load-bearing:** PG skips an already-`convalidated` descendant entirely; goopg flips nothing on such a child but still *recurses through* it — PG can prune because its flat `find_all_inheritors` list already contains the whole tree, so a validated intermediate node can never hide a still-unvalidated grandchild behind it, whereas goopg's level-at-a-time descent would let exactly that happen if the skip also pruned the walk. Same end state, different traversal. A descendant missing the constraint is a **skip**, not an error: goopg's catalog can legitimately reach that state and PG's `elog` there is an internal assertion, so promoting it would invent a new failure mode. Confirmed not a risk: the row-scan backing SET NOT NULL already descends the full tree (`forEachLiveRow` → `allDescendants` → `forEachLiveRowRel`), so the cascade cannot introduce a false-negative `23502`. Guards (`internal/executor/operators_ddl_notnull_validate_cascade_test.go`, FAIL-pre verified against reverted source / PASS-post): 3-level plain inheritance (`src/test/regress/sql/constraints.sql:908-917`), partitioned tree (`:920-939`), already-validated-descendant no-drift (`InhCount`/`IsLocal` untouched), and both entry points. Design §42. Gates: `go build ./...` PASS, `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/catalog/` PASS, named guards PASS (re-run by the coordinator before commit), regress `constraints` 216/11 → 188/9, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS, pgbench smoke via the hook. Two deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19 (the CHECK-constraint VALIDATE cascade has the identical gap — and PG's CHECK version has NO already-convalidated skip, `tablecmds.c:13117-13210`; and PG's `ONLY`-with-children `ERRCODE_INVALID_TABLE_DEFINITION` guard at `:13260-13263`, unreproducible today because neither call site plumbs an ONLY/recurse flag this deep).
  - [x] **M0134-0005an — the CHECK twin of 0005ak, and the word `NO INHERIT` that nobody was reading** — **LANDED 2026-08-19.** First slice in this run whose witness is **not** `constraints.sql`: that diff enters and exits at **176 lines / 8 hunks, unchanged and predicted to be unchanged** — all five of `constraints.sql`'s `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` sites (`:864,:866,:904,:934,:947`) target NOT NULL, which 0005ak already closed. The witness is `alter_table.sql:397-428` (that file's diff is a pre-existing 3781 lines / 110 hunks; not a meaningful per-slice metric yet). **The 0005ak ledger row was right about the gap and wrong about its size:** it predicted a materially larger change because CHECK validation "implies re-scanning each descendant's rows" — but M0134-0005ac had already made `forEachLiveRow` (`:12850`) walk `allDescendants`, and `validateCheckConstraintRows` (`:12954`) is one of its callers, so the scan half had been quietly correct for two slices and only the catalog flag cascade was missing. That made this the **smaller** twin, not the larger. **The more interesting defect found on the way:** PG's `QueueCheckConstraintValidation` (`tablecmds.c:13109-13210`) makes exactly ONE decision about the tree, at `:13140` — `if (!recursing && !con->connoinherit) children = find_all_inheritors(...)` — and because PG queues its Phase-3 row scan **per relation** off that same list, `connoinherit` gates the scan too. **One flag, both halves.** goopg had the flag (`catalog.NamedCheckConstraint.NoInherit`, `catalog.go:221`) and consulted it nowhere here, so it got both halves wrong in opposite directions: never cascaded, and always descended when scanning. The second is the live bug — `alter_table.sql:412-425` exists to pin it (`check (a = 2) no inherit not valid` must validate against the parent's rows only; goopg failed on the child's `a=1`). Fix (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`, +92/−5): `validateCheckConstraintRows` takes a trailing `noInherit bool` and scans `forEachLiveRowRel(tbl,tbl,…)` instead of `forEachLiveRow` when set; **both** callers pass it (`:8619` VALIDATE, `:8735` ADD CHECK — the Rule-#2 pair, and why `ADD CHECK … NO INHERIT` is fixed too); new `cascadeCheckValidateToDescendants` (`:12352`) is a near-verbatim copy of 0005ak's `cascadeNotNullValidateToDescendants` (`:12306`) matching by constraint **name** (`cascadeCheckDropToChildren`'s key, `:12191`) instead of column name, called under `!NoInherit`. **Divergence, inherited from 0005ak and still load-bearing:** PG's CHECK path has no already-convalidated skip at all (no analog of the NN version's `:13277-13278`), so goopg recurses unconditionally — but for a different reason than PG's, namely that goopg has no one-shot `find_all_inheritors` and a prune on an intermediate node would hide its grandchildren; the `NotValid` guard wraps the flip only, never the descent. Guards: `operators_ddl_check_validate_cascade_test.go`, 10 subtests, FAIL-pre proven by stashing only the source file. Design §45. **Four rows ledgered 2026-08-19**, three of them found while writing the tests: (a) **`DELETE FROM ONLY <t>` does not honour `ONLY`** — deletes descendant rows too; newly surfaced, no prior row, a DML correctness bug and the widest blast radius of the three (it is also why the NO INHERIT scan test could not be a literal port and was split into two fixtures); (b) `catalog.AddCheckInherited` (`catalog.go:305`) stamps `NotValid=false` unconditionally, so a child inherits a `NOT VALID` parent CHECK **pre-validated** and this slice's new cascade has nothing to fix — the M0134-0005z row owns the `NotEnforced` half of the same helper's blindness, land them together; (c) PG's `ONLY`/recurse guard (`:13146-13150`) has no plumbing on `AlterTableValidateConstraint`; (d) PG skips the row re-scan of an already-`convalidated` descendant, goopg re-scans the whole tree in one pass (observable via `alter_table.sql:408-411`'s `boo()` NOTICE). Also re-confirmed with a second independent sighting: `AlterTableAddCheck` propagates only to `PartitionChildren`, never `InheritanceChildren` (M0134-0005ac row).
  - [x] **M0134-0005aq — the partition walk that stopped one level down** — **LANDED 2026-08-19.** Discharges the deferral row 0005ap filed the same day and is the campaign's third **DML** correctness fix. On a sub-partitioned table (root → intermediate partition that is itself `PARTITION BY` → leaf), **all four** DML forms reported `0` rows and left the leaf untouched, with no error: `UPDATE root_p SET …`, `DELETE FROM root_p`, `UPDATE root_p … FROM other`, `DELETE FROM root_p USING other` — each `1` on PG 18.3, live-verified side by side. `SELECT` saw the rows the whole time, which is what kept the gap invisible. **Root cause:** `catalog.InMemory.PartitionChildren` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:4510`) returns direct children only, and all four expansion sites in `internal/executor/operators_storage.go` called it raw, so the fan-out list held the storage-less intermediate node and never the leaf. The hierarchy's other two consumers had each already solved this independently — INSERT routing via `routeToPartitionDepth` (`:3014`) and the read path via `collectAllPartitionLeaves` (`internal/optimizer/planner.go:3350`) — leaving DML the last flat consumer. **Upstream cannot express it:** expansion is ONE site, `expand_inherited_rtentry` (`postgres/src/backend/optimizer/util/inherit.c:86`), and it recurses; the **sixth** consecutive instance of this campaign's shape. Fix is +42 lines, one file: new `collectDMLPartitionLeaves` (`:2869`) — BFS, **partitions-only** (inheritance children stay a separate branch per site), **leaves-only** (a node with a non-empty `PartitionKey` is recursed into, never scanned, since intermediate nodes have no storage and must not be heap-locked), `seen` cycle guard — swapped in at all four sites (`updateOp.Next:5166`, `deleteOp.Next:6204`, `updateOp.updateWithFrom:6655`, `deleteOp.deleteWithUsing:7180`) with the `!o.plan.Only` gate, the `colMap: nil` partition split and the per-child `RowExclusiveLock` all untouched. **`allDescendants` (`operators_fk.go:1004`) was deliberately NOT reused** — it mixes in inheritance children, returns non-leaf nodes, and filters on `transam.CurrentPartitionDetachEpoch()`, a globally-current epoch that is intentionally FK/RI-specific (`operators_fk.go:1036-1049`); the new helper filters on the statement's own `ctx.Snap.PartitionDetachEpoch` via `catalog.VisiblePartitionChildren`, mirroring `:3008`. Side effect worth naming: the four DML sites previously applied **no** detach-visibility filter at all, so a concurrently detached partition is now correctly excluded from the fan-out too. Guards: `internal/executor/operators_dml_subpartition_test.go` (new, 6 tests) — one per broken form asserting both the count and the **leaf table queried by its own name**, plus a one-level regression guard and an `UPDATE ONLY` fan-out-suppression guard; FAIL-pre proven by stashing only the source file (all four reported `RowsAffected=0`, leaf unchanged). Gates: 6/6 new PASS, §47's four `TestDeleteUsingPartitioned*` + `TestAlterTablePartitionDescendantRecursion` still PASS, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units` PASS, `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35). Design §48. **Metric: `constraints.sql` enters and exits at 176 lines / 8 hunks** — it never writes a sub-partitioned DML, so no per-slice diff number is claimed. **DEFERRED (ledgered 2026-08-19):** the detached-**grandchild** epoch path has no test — the DML fixture helpers cannot express two statements observing different snapshot epochs around a live `DETACH PARTITION CONCURRENTLY`.
  - [x] **M0134-0005at — `ONLY` was ignored, and the parent disagreed with its own children about the constraint's name** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **176 lines / 8 hunks** `constraints.sql` baseline, which it does NOT move (that file exercises no `ONLY`+`ADD CHECK` case at all — the slice is paid for by correctness and by retiring `alter_table.sql:2862-2877`). Discharges **both** deferrals ledgered by 0005as, in one file, +14/−1 of production code. **(A) The `ONLY` refusal.** `AlterTableAddCheck` never read `s.Only`, so `ALTER TABLE ONLY p ADD CONSTRAINT c CHECK (…)` **cascaded to every child anyway** — note this is the *opposite* of what the 0005as ledger row predicted ("silently adds to the parent alone"); the row was written from the PG semantics, not from a repro, and the real goopg behaviour was worse. PG does not narrow the statement, it **refuses** it: `ATAddCheckNNConstraint` raises 42P16 / `constraint must be added to child tables too` (no detail, no hint) at `postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:10020-10023`, because a parent-only CHECK cannot be represented in the catalog without leaving the tree permanently inconsistent. Fix: `if s.Only && !act.NoInherit && o.hasInheritanceChildren(tbl)` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go:8729`), mirroring the ADD COLUMN twin at `:11053-11054`. **Two orderings are load-bearing:** the guard sits AFTER the NO INHERIT gate (PG's `is_no_inherit` return at `:10004` precedes the ONLY refusal at `:10020`, so `ONLY … NO INHERIT` on a table with children is *accepted*), and before any catalog mutation, so a rejected statement leaves both parent and children untouched. ONE rule covers partitioned and plain-inheritance parents — PG makes a single `find_inheritance_children` call here; the `ATExecSetNotNull` variant (`:8017-8028`) that *does* split them and adds an errhint is a different function and a standing trap in this area, explicitly not copied. **(B) The parent's own name.** 0005as hoisted `o.autoCheckName` resolution for the cascade but left `:8759` passing the raw `act.ConstraintName` to BOTH `AddCheckFull` and `allocConstraintOID` — and since `allocConstraintOID` returns 0 for an empty name (`:4431-4436`), an anonymous `ALTER TABLE t ADD CHECK (x > 0)` left the parent with `Name==""` **and `OID==0`** while every descendant carried a proper `<table>_<col>_check` and a real OID: the tree disagreed with itself. Both arguments now take `conName`. `autoCheckName` already reproduced `ChooseConstraintName` (`heap.c:2548-2575`) correctly — the bug was purely which value reached the store, which is why 0005as's own `TestCheckAddCascadeAnonymousNameResolved` passed while asserting only the CHILD side. Guards: new `internal/executor/operators_ddl_check_add_only_test.go` (4 tests) porting **both** arms of `alter_table.sql:2862-2877` — the reject case (`:2862-2863`, partitioned parent WITH partitions) and the **allow** case (`:2873-2877`, parent with none yet), the latter being what proves the guard is not over-broad and the arm a narrower port would have skipped — plus the plain-INHERITS reject arm and the `ONLY … NO INHERIT` accept arm; and `TestCheckAddCascadeAnonymousNameResolved` extended with a parent-side assertion. FAIL-pre proven for all three by stashing only `operators_ddl.go`. **One existing test flipped by design:** `TestC9DropConstraintOnlyDoesNotCascade` (`operators_ddl_c9_residuals_final_test.go:276-284`) seeded its state with `ALTER TABLE ONLY p ADD CONSTRAINT check_b …`, which must now error — its own comment already flagged this seam ("goopg's ONLY-guard for ADD CONSTRAINT is a separate ledgered item"); the `ONLY` was dropped from that seed line only, assertions untouched. Design: `docs/design/0096-0009-table-inheritance.md`, the 2026-08-19 addendum's new items 5-6 (which replace its "still unimplemented" note). Gates: build+vet clean; 4/4 new PASS; 11/11 `TestCheckAddCascade|TestCheckInheritFlags|TestC9DropConstraintOnly` PASS; `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units` PASS (7m45; `internal/initdb` 412s + `cmd/goopg` 74s re-ran on the pending diff's dependency hash, not a regression); `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35); pgbench smoke via the hook. **DEFERRED (ledgered 2026-08-19):** PG's parent-level constraint **merge** short-circuit (`tablecmds.c:9998-9999`) returns before computing `children` when the relation already holds an identical constraint — and it precedes the ONLY refusal, so `ALTER TABLE ONLY p ADD CONSTRAINT c CHECK (…)` on a parent that ALREADY holds `c` must succeed silently where goopg now raises 42P16.**
  - [x] **M0134-0005as — the ALTER-time CHECK cascade reached partitions only, one level deep, under an empty name** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **176 lines / 8 hunks** baseline left by 0005ar. Discharges that row's *blocking* deferral: `AlterTableAddCheck`'s cascade (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go:8752-8781`) was a flat loop over `im3.PartitionChildren(tbl.OID)`, so `ALTER TABLE p ADD CONSTRAINT c CHECK (…)` reached **no plain-INHERITS child at all** and **no grandchild of any kind** — which is why 0005ar's literal port of `alter_table.sql:397-406` passed vacuously: the children never got a `NamedChecks` entry to get wrong. PG oracle `ATAddCheckNNConstraint` (`postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:9911-10049`, shared by CHECK and NOT NULL) resolves the descendant set with `find_inheritance_children` — **one level per call**, then DFS recursion at `:10028-10046`; `pg_inherits` already unifies plain inheritance and partition attachment, so one call covers both kinds. Fix: new `cascadeCheckToChildren`/`cascadeCheckToChildrenAt` placed beside their DROP-direction sibling `cascadeCheckDropToChildren` (`:12220`) and copying the ADD-direction NOT-NULL twin `cascadeNotNullToChildren` verbatim in shape — `collectInheritanceAndPartitionChildren` (`:12187`) per level, per-**EDGE** `visited map[[2]uint32]bool`, `maxNotNullCascadeDepth` bound. **The per-edge guard is not a detail:** `allDescendants` (`operators_fk.go:1004`) has the identical reachability set and was the obvious reuse candidate, but it dedups per **node**, discarding exactly the information PG's `coninhcount` bookkeeping counts once per inheritance EDGE (`heap.c:2774-2845`) — a diamond descendant is legitimately visited twice. **Two latent bugs surfaced by the widening, both fixed here.** (1) The missing `NoInherit` early return: PG returns at `:10004` **before enumerating any children**, and goopg had no such gate — accidentally safe only because the partitions-only walk was empty whenever `NoInherit` held (`NoInherit` + partitioned already errors 42P16 at `:8726`). Adding inheritance children destroys that accident, so the gate is now explicit. (2) The **empty constraint name**: the cascade passed the raw `act.ConstraintName`, so an anonymous `ADD CHECK (x > 0)` propagated `""` to every child; PG fills `newConstraint->conname` once at the top and reuses that node, so the resolution via `o.autoCheckName` is hoisted and now shared by the validation scan and the cascade. Guards (new `operators_ddl_check_add_cascade_test.go`, 5 tests): `TestCheckAddCascade{ThreeLevelPlainInherits,MixedTree,NoInheritDoesNotPropagate,AnonymousNameResolved,DiamondTerminates}` — FAIL-pre proven for the three-level, mixed-tree and anonymous-name cases by stashing only `operators_ddl.go`. **`TestCheckInheritFlagsPortedAlterTableInheritedNotValid` flipped from a vacuous pin into a real FAIL-pre guard** (round 2): its stale doc comment is rewritten and it now asserts `cif_attmp6`/`cif_attmp7` carry `b_le_20` with `NotValid=true, IsLocal=false, InhCount=1`. Design: `docs/design/0096-0009-table-inheritance.md` 2026-08-19 addendum, which also tabulates the **four** coexisting descendant walks and their deliberately different node sets (goopg still has no one-shot `find_all_inheritors`). Gates: build+vet clean, 5/5 new PASS, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units` PASS (`internal/initdb` 412s cold-cache, not a regression), `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35), pgbench smoke via the hook. **Two deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19:** PG's `ONLY`-with-existing-children rejection (`errmsg("constraint must be added to child tables too")`, `:10020-10023`) is still absent — goopg silently adds to the parent alone; and the parent's own `AddCheckFull` still stores the raw (possibly empty) `act.ConstraintName`, so parent and children now disagree on the name for an anonymous ALTER-added check.
  - [x] **M0134-0005ar — one helper, two flags, three call sites, and only ONE of them symmetric** — **LANDED 2026-08-19.** Discharges BOTH open halves of `catalog.Table.AddCheckInherited`'s blindness — the M0134-0005z ledger row (`NotEnforced`, `:1540`) and the M0134-0005an row (`NotValid`, `:1573`), which had each deferred with the same instruction: land them together, since the fix is one signature change across three call sites both briefs had fenced off. The helper (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:305`) threaded only `Name/Expr/OID/IsLocal/InhCount` and hard-zeroed both flags, so **every** CHECK goopg propagated to an inheritance or partition child was born enforced and pre-validated regardless of the parent. **Where both ledger rows were wrong:** they predicted a symmetric fix ("copy the parent's flags at all three sites"). PG has **two** rules and they split on whether the child existed when the constraint was declared. CREATE-time (`INHERITS`, `PARTITION OF`, both via `MergeAttributes` → `MergeCheckConstraint`, `postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:3167-3222`) sets `newcon->is_enforced = is_enforced; newcon->skip_validation = !is_enforced;` at `:3219-3220` — validity derives **purely from enforcement**, never from the parent's `convalidated`, because a fresh child is empty and any enforced constraint is trivially satisfied (which is also why upstream has no regress case for it). ALTER-time (`ATAddCheckNNConstraint`, `tablecmds.c:9912-10049`, recursing at `:10042-10043`) reuses the **same `Constraint` node** for every child, so each gets the user's literal clause unchanged and Phase 3 queues an independent per-child validation (`:9956-9966`) precisely because those children may already hold violating rows. Fix (+35/−12, two files): helper widened to `(name, expr, oid, notValid, notEnforced)` — matching its sibling `AddCheckFull` (`:295`), which had carried all three flags from the start, making `AddCheckInherited` simply the stale narrow twin — then `execCreateTable`'s INHERITS loop (`operators_ddl.go:4049`) and `execCreatePartitionChild` (`:5175`) pass `(parent.NotEnforced, parent.NotEnforced)` while `AlterTableAddCheck`'s cascade (`:8765`) passes `(act.NotValid, act.CheckNotEnforced)` through unchanged; 0005z's post-hoc stamp at `:4050-4055` deleted. **Seventh consecutive Rule-#2 sighting:** `execCreatePartitionChild` carries a comment at `:4026` asserting it mirrors the INHERITS loop, and it did not — site 1 had 0005z's stamp, site 2 had nothing at all, drifted for a whole slice. Guards: `internal/executor/operators_ddl_check_inherit_flags_test.go` (new, 5 tests, 3 FAIL-pre proven by stashing only the two source files), including `TestCheckInheritFlagsCreateTimeAntiSymmetryNotValid/{INHERITS,PARTITION_OF}` — an **anti-symmetry pin** that exists specifically to stop a future loop from "tidying" the three sites into one rule. Gates: 5/5 new PASS, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units` PASS (~7m, `internal/initdb`+`cmd/goopg` cold — cache cold-start, not a regression), `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35), build+vet clean. Design §49. **Metric: `constraints.sql` enters and exits at 176 lines / 8 hunks** — that file exercises neither a `NOT VALID` nor a `NOT ENFORCED` inherited CHECK; the slice is paid for by correctness and by unblocking `alter_table.sql:397-428`. **DEFERRED (ledgered 2026-08-19):** `AlterTableAddCheck`'s cascade still walks `PartitionChildren` ONLY, never `InheritanceChildren`, so the new ALTER-time flag propagation is reachable only on partition children — the retained literal port of `alter_table.sql:397-406` passes **vacuously** for that reason, and that gap (0005z row, second clause) is now the *blocking* prerequisite for the fixture rather than a parallel one.
  - [x] **M0134-0005ap — `DELETE … USING` was the one expansion site that forgot partitions** — **LANDED 2026-08-19.** Discharges the deferral row 0005ao filed the same day (`.ralph/deferral_ledger.md`) and is the campaign's second **DML** correctness fix. `DELETE FROM <partitioned_parent> USING <other> WHERE …` silently deleted **zero** rows from every partition: affected-row count 0, no error, data left in place. Plain `DELETE`, `UPDATE` and `UPDATE … FROM` were all correct — goopg writes descendant expansion out **four separate times** in `internal/executor/operators_storage.go` (`updateOp.Next:5126`, `deleteOp.Next:6165`, `updateOp.updateWithFrom:6605`, `deleteOp.deleteWithUsing:7132`) and three of the four append BOTH `PartitionChildren` and `AccessibleInheritanceChildren`; `deleteWithUsing` appended only the inheritance children. No planner-side pre-expansion compensates: `usingScanTargets` is seeded solely inside the function (`:7137`, the parent alone), so the partitions genuinely never reached victim collection. **Upstream has exactly ONE such site** — `expand_inherited_rtentry` (`postgres/src/backend/optimizer/util/inherit.c:86`) expands any RTE whose `rte->inh` is set, assigned once at parse/rewrite time regardless of whether the RTE is the DML target, a `FROM` item or a `USING` item, and `nodeModifyTable.c` has NO USING-specific partition branch. PG cannot express this bug; goopg's 4x duplication is what makes it expressible, and 0005ao's gating of all four sites for `ONLY` is precisely what exposed the asymmetry — the fifth consecutive instance of this campaign's shape (the behaviour exists, the grep hits, but on the sibling). Fix is **+12 lines, one file**: a loop over `imDel.PartitionChildren(tbl.OID)` inside the **existing** `!o.plan.Only` guard (`:7140`, added by 0005ao), taking `acquireRelLock(..., lmgr.RowExclusiveLock)` per child. `colMap` is deliberately **nil** for partition children and `buildInheritColMap` for inheritance children — a partition shares the parent's column ordinals, an inheritance child need not; all three sibling sites already make that split, and RETURNING needs nothing extra because a nil `colMap` means `oldRow` is already parent-aligned. Putting the loop INSIDE the existing `if` rather than a new one is what keeps `DELETE FROM ONLY <partitioned_parent> USING …` a no-op. The EPQ retry loop (`:7248+`) needed no change: it is generic over `victim{rel,blk,slot,cols}` and `epqSlotMovedToAnotherPartition` (`:7321`) keys off `vRel`/`v.blk`/`v.slot`, so a partition-sourced victim — including one moved to a sibling partition under RC — flows through unmodified, as `deleteOp.Next` already proved. Guards: `internal/executor/operators_dml_using_partition_test.go` (new, 4 tests) — `TestDeleteUsingPartitionedTarget` (victims in more than one partition, each partition queried **by its own name** so a parent-level read cannot mask a miss), `TestDeleteUsingPartitionedReturning`, `TestDeleteFromOnlyUsingPartitionedNoop`, `TestDeleteUsingInheritNoPartitionRegression`; FAIL-pre proven by reverting only `operators_storage.go` and keeping the tests (the two partition-targeting tests failed with `RowsAffected=0` / `0 rows returned`, the `ONLY` and inheritance guards stayed green). Design §47. **Metric: `constraints.sql` enters and exits at 176 lines / 8 hunks** — it never writes `DELETE … USING`, so no per-slice diff number is claimed; this is a silent wrong-answer bug harvested by the campaign, not a diff-line fix. **DEFERRED (ledgered 2026-08-19):** `catalog.InMemory.PartitionChildren` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:4510`) is **flat — direct children only** — so all four DML expansion sites miss **grandchild** partitions under multi-level sub-partitioning; pre-existing and common to all four, the DML analogue of the FK-side row M0134-0005h. No multi-level capability test was written on purpose.
  - [x] **M0134-0005ao — `ONLY` was parsed, planned away, and never reached the executor** — **LANDED 2026-08-19.** Discharges the deferral row 0005an filed hours earlier (`.ralph/deferral_ledger.md:1572`) and is the campaign's first **DML** correctness fix. `constraints.sql` never writes `DELETE FROM ONLY`, so its metric enters and exits at **176 lines / 8 hunks**; the witness is `alter_table.sql:412-425`, whose own diff (3781 lines / 110 hunks, pre-existing) is too coarse for a single-hunk signal — **no per-slice diff number is claimed**, deliberately. **The defect class is the interesting part: the feature looked implemented from both ends.** `ONLY` has always parsed into `parser.RangeVar.Only` (`ast.go:658`, set by the shared `parseRangeVar`, `select.go:1373,1476`, reached by both DML targets at `dml.go:390,556`), and the executor's descendant expansion has always been correct *for the no-`ONLY` case*. Neither end was wrong — **the wire between them did not exist**: `optimizer.Update` (`plan.go:2041`) and `optimizer.Delete` (`:2070`) had no field to carry the flag, so `planUpdate` (`planner.go:10721`) / `planDelete` (`:10906`) never read `s.Target.Only`. No syntax error, no red test, and grepping `Only` returns plenty of hits, none of them the bug. **Rule #2 applied twice over:** goopg writes the expansion out separately in FOUR executor sites — `updateOp.Next` (`operators_storage.go:5126`), `updateOp.updateWithFrom` (`:6613`), `deleteOp.Next` (`:6165`), `deleteOp.deleteWithUsing` (`:7135`) — and the planner has FOUR matching construction sites (two per verb: plain + cross-product). Gating any three of four yields a silently half-correct `ONLY`. Fix is +20/−8 over three files: `Only bool` on both plan nodes, `Only: s.Target.Only` at all four planner sites, `&& !o.plan.Only` at all four executor sites. **Partitioning and inheritance are gated together, not selectively**, because upstream has exactly one flag and one expansion site: `gram.y:13922-13950` sets `RangeVar.inh`, and `expand_inherited_rtentry` (`postgres/src/backend/optimizer/util/inherit.c:86`) is PG's SOLE descendant-RTE site, gated on it — a partitioned parent takes the identical path. `ONLY` on a partitioned table is legal and simply matches nothing, so **no error path was added**; two guards pin that no-op-not-error behaviour. The correct precedent already existed twice one subsystem over — `planner.go:3047`'s `!rv.Only` gate for SELECT and `truncateTableAndPartitions(..., only)` (`operators_ddl.go:15118,15244,15280`) for `TRUNCATE ONLY`; SELECT and TRUNCATE were right the whole time, only DML was wrong. Guards: `internal/executor/operators_dml_only_test.go` (new, 277 lines, 8 tests), each asserting BOTH surviving parent/child rows AND the affected-row count; FAIL-pre proven by stashing only the three source files (the six `ONLY` tests failed, the two no-`ONLY` regression guards stayed green). Design §46. **Two rows ledgered 2026-08-19:** (a) `deleteOp.deleteWithUsing` never fans out to `PartitionChildren` **at all, independently of `ONLY`** — `DELETE FROM <partitioned_parent> USING …` misses every partition, while the other three sites append both child kinds; a DML correctness bug in its own right, found precisely by gating all four; (b) 0005an's split `parent_noinh_convalid` fixture (`operators_ddl_check_validate_cascade_test.go:212-226`) can now be re-joined into a literal upstream port, deferred as cosmetic test-fidelity debt rather than churn a passing 10-subtest guard.
  - [x] **M0134-0005al — a PRIMARY KEY column could be made nullable** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **188 / 9** baseline left by 0005ak; exits at **176 lines / 8 hunks** (−12 lines, −1 hunk, no new `@@`, no existing hunk grew). `ALTER TABLE notnull_tbl2 ALTER a DROP NOT NULL` on a PK column returned success and cleared the flag, leaving a **nullable PRIMARY KEY** — a catalog-integrity hole, not a message-wording hunk like most of the remaining constraints tail. **The guard was not overlooked, it was implemented on the wrong twin:** M0134-0005 Bucket 3 (2026-08-18) ported PG's PK-membership refusal into the DROP-a-NOT-NULL-**by-name** path (`execAlterTableDropConstraint`) and its own ledger row recorded the by-column arm as "equally unguarded (Rule #2)" to fix in the same pass — it was not, so goopg refused `DROP CONSTRAINT <nn-name>` on a PK column while accepting the `ALTER COLUMN a DROP NOT NULL` spelling of the identical operation. Third occurrence of this campaign's recurring shape: the guard exists, the reviewer greps, the grep hits — on the sibling. PG holds the check not in `ATExecDropNotNull` (`tablecmds.c:7742`) but in `dropconstraint_internal` (called `:7817`), whose `CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL` branch carries **four** ordered refusals at `:14128-14181` with PK **first** — ahead of the replica-identity check (`:14161`), the identity-column check (`:14169`) and `attnotnull` being cleared (`:14184`). Fix: walk `IndexesOnTable(tbl, NamespaceDBOid(…))` for `idx.Primary`/`idx.Columns` in the `case parser.AlterTableDropNotNull:` arm (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go:10491`), before `clearNotNullConstraint` so the catalog is untouched on rejection — placed in the case arm, NOT the helper, which has three callers. `42P16` / `column "%s" is in a primary key`, no detail, no hint (**not** the `55000` used by the neighbouring `verifyNotNullPKCompatible` — same family, different PG errcode). Pins: four `TestAlterTableDropNotNullOnPrimaryKey*` tests, all FAIL-pre/PASS-post (rejection; catalog-unchanged-after-rejection; non-PK sibling still droppable; each member of a multi-column PK rejected independently), plus a wire-level psql SQLSTATE/message check on a cgroup-capped throwaway server. Design §43. **DEFERRED (ledgered 2026-08-19, 2nd time for this pair):** goopg now runs 1 of PG's 4 `dropconstraint_internal` NOT NULL refusals; the replica-identity-index (`:14162-14167`) and identity-column (`:14174-14181`) refusals are still absent from **both** spellings and must land together in one pass. **Trap flagged for the next slice:** the `ADD GENERATED … AS IDENTITY` hunk sits in the same tail hunk and looks ~2 lines, but `ALTER COLUMN … ADD GENERATED` does not parse in goopg at all (no parser branch, no action kind, no executor case — a silent no-op), so closing it means porting `ATExecAddIdentity` (`tablecmds.c:8240-8362`), a feature slice not a guard; already ledgered three times (0005n/0005v/0005w) — do not re-estimate it from the diff's line count.
  - [x] **M0134-0005am — the other two refusals PG makes before a NOT NULL may be dropped** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **176 / 8** baseline left by 0005al; exits at **176 / 8, unchanged — the predicted result**. Neither refusal is witnessed by any statement in `src/test/regress/sql/constraints.sql` (confirmed by grep), so this slice moves the diff by zero lines; it was selected because the **ledger** asked for it twice, not because the fixture did — a regress-diff campaign that only lands what the fixture witnesses accumulates exactly the half-ported guard that produced 0005al. PG's `dropconstraint_internal` `CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL` branch (`postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:14109-14191`) makes **four** ordered decisions — refuse if the column is in the PRIMARY KEY (`:14128-14159`), refuse if it is in the index used as replica identity (`:14161-14167`), refuse if it is an identity column (`:14169-14181`), and only then clear `attnotnull` (`:14183-14188`). 0005al landed refusal 1 on both spellings; refusals **2 and 3 were absent from both**, so goopg silently dropped a NOT NULL PG protects. Landed on **both** twins in ONE pass (Rule #2 — this pair was ledgered twice, 2026-08-18 Bucket 3 and 2026-08-19 0005al, precisely because a prior slice fixed one side only): `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`, `case parser.AlterTableDropNotNull:` (column from `act.ColumnName`) and the "3.7. NOT NULL constraints" branch of `execAlterTableDropConstraint` (column from `nc.ColName`, **not** `act.ColumnName` — the constraint is matched by name and carries its own column), each inserted after that site's existing PK-membership loop and before `clearNotNullConstraint`, so PG's ordering holds on both and the catalog is untouched on every rejection. Twin B's header comment, which asserted these two refusals were "NOT implemented — out of scope", was corrected in the same hunk. **The two messages are deliberately asymmetric and a Go test that only asserts "an error occurred" cannot see it:** refusal 2 is `42P16` (`ERRCODE_INVALID_TABLE_DEFINITION`, shared with the PK refusal) naming only the column, `column "%s" is in index used as replica identity`; refusal 3 is `55000` (`ERRCODE_OBJECT_NOT_IN_PREREQUISITE_STATE`) naming the column **and** the relation, `column "%s" of relation "%s" is an identity column` — so the pins assert exact SQLSTATE *and* exact text. Membership reads `catalog.Index.IsReplicaIdentity` and `catalog.Column.IdentityColumn` (both `ALWAYS` and `BY DEFAULT` refuse, matching PG's `attidentity != '\0'`); no catalog field, parser branch or helper signature was added. **One documented divergence, load-bearing to read the guard correctly:** PG derives the replica-identity attribute set from `rd_replidindex`, which `RelationGetIndexList` (`relcache.c:4938-4945`) sets to the **PRIMARY KEY index** for `relreplident='d'` — so in PG refusal 2 is exactly redundant with refusal 1 in the default mode; goopg sets `IsReplicaIdentity` only for explicit `USING INDEX` (`operators_ddl.go:9940-9981` resolves `chosenIdx` only when `mode=="i"`), so goopg's refusal 2 fires only there. **End behavior is identical** (for `'d'` the PK refusal already fired first; for `'f'`/`'n'` PG's `rd_replidindex` is `InvalidOid` and neither fires), and the reasoning is recorded in-code because the two implementations reach the same answer by different routes. Also corrected for the record: the M0119-0004 ledger claim that `REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX` is rejected with `0A000` is **stale** — the parser accepts it and the executor sets the flag, which is what makes refusal 2 reachable rather than dead code. Pins (7, all FAIL-pre verified by reverting the guard hunk / PASS-post): 5 beside the `TestAlterTableDropNotNullOnPrimaryKey*` family in `operators_ddl_drop_constraint_cascade_test.go` — replica-identity refusal, catalog-unchanged-after-rejection, identity-column refusal, **PK-beats-identity ordering** on a column that is both, and a sibling-column-still-drops regression guard — plus 2 subtests beside `NotNullPkMemberRefused` in `operators_fk_unique_drop_constraint_test.go` for the by-name twin. The ordering test is the one that catches a future refactor reordering the guards: three independently-correct checks can still emit the wrong message for a column that trips two. Design §44. Gates: `go build ./...` PASS, `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/catalog/` PASS, named guards PASS (re-run by the coordinator before commit), `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS, regress `constraints` **176 / 8 unchanged**, pgbench smoke via the hook. No tpch-spotcheck — DDL guards only, no planner/executor row-path change. **Closes** the twice-ledgered pair (both rows flipped to `resolved`); one NEW deferral ledgered 2026-08-19: goopg has no notion of an implicit default (`'d'`) replica-identity index, harmless for this guard but wrong for any future consumer of the replica-identity attribute set (pgoutput row identity, `indisreplident` reporting).
  - [x] **M0134-0005ah — one output the psql client asked to be *pretty*, and one catalog field `ONLY` was never allowed to touch** — **LANDED 2026-08-19** at the **290 lines / 15 hunks** baseline left by 0005ag; exits at **257 / 13** (−33 lines, −2 hunks, no new `@@`). Two residues 0005ag surfaced, both ledgered under that task-id, and research (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005ah-research/report.md`) corrected the framing of BOTH. **A — `pg_get_constraintdef(oid, pretty)` ignored argument 1.** goopg printed `CHECK ((a > 0))` where `\d+` shows `CHECK (a > 0)`; the obvious reading ("one paren too many") is WRONG and acting on it would have regressed pg_dump. PG emits both spellings and the CALLER picks: `ruleutils.c:pg_oper_expr` (~:10735-10769) self-wraps every `OpExpr` only when `!PRETTY_PAREN`, psql `\d+` always passes `pretty=true` (`src/bin/psql/describe.c:2530`), pg_dump passes `false`/the 1-arg form (`pg_dump.c:7768`, `ruleutils.c:2152`). goopg's dispatch arm (`internal/executor/expr.go`) never read the argument, so every consumer got the non-pretty form. Fix threads `pretty` into `renderCheckPredicate`/`renderDomainCheckPredicate` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`), stripping one self-wrap layer for a bare top-level binary comparison. Shared `defaultExprToSQL` deliberately UNTOUCHED — its other consumers (index predicates, column defaults, partition keys) are all non-pretty. The retained non-pretty assertions are the pg_dump regression guard. Compound AND/OR under pretty is out of scope (PG drops parens at every level, `alter_table.out:3413`) and is **pinned by a test** so the boundary cannot move silently; ledgered. **B — `ALTER TABLE ONLY … DROP` must still walk children.** goopg read "ONLY must not propagate" as "must not touch children at all"; PG reads it as "do not recursively DELETE the child's row, but DO fix its bookkeeping" — `dropconstraint_internal` (`postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:14300-14343`) always walks children, decrements `coninhcount`, flips `conislocal=true` at zero. Skipping the walk left real `pg_constraint` corruption whose symptom is a **spurious `(inherited)` tag** in `\d+` — note the 0005ag ledger row described goopg as *omitting* the tag, which was inverted; it is ONE bug, previously ledgered as G4 under 0005ae, not two. Three sites, one rule (Hard-won Rule #2 as "direct target vs recursion target"): `execAlterTableDropConstraint` CHECK branch and NOT-NULL-by-name branch (both the `if isIM && !only` guard, whose in-code comment was inverted) plus `AlterTableDropNotNull` (no cascade at all). All three now call new `cascadeCheckDropToChildren`/`cascadeNotNullDropToChildren`, reusing the ADD-direction twin `cascadeNotNullToChildrenAt`'s edge-keyed visited set + depth backstop (diamond safety) instead of a second traversal. **PG's asymmetry preserved verbatim, not tidied away:** the recurse branch deletes an orphan-to-be outright; only the `ONLY` branch sets `conislocal` at zero. The pre-existing `TestC9DropConstraintOnlyDoesNotCascade` had pinned the BUG (child untouched, `InhCount==1`, no `conislocal` check) — its P1 assertion is corrected in place (row present, `InhCount==0`, `IsLocal==true`) and recorded rather than silently changed; P2 was already right. Multi-level: with an inherited mid-level copy the drop is REFUSED 42P16 by the pre-existing top-of-function guard, so the test pins the refusal rather than inventing an unverified grandchild cascade (ledgered). Guards (FAIL-pre/PASS-post, 5 of 6 red pre-fix; the multi-level refusal correctly unaffected): `TestRenderCheckPredicatePretty`, `TestRenderDomainCheckPredicatePretty`, and `internal/executor/operators_ddl_drop_constraint_cascade_test.go`'s `TestAlterTableOnlyDropConstraint{WalksChildren,ByNameWalksChildren,MultiLevelInheritance}`, `TestAlterTableOnlyAlterColumnDropNotNullWalksChildren`, `TestAlterTableRecursing{DropConstraintDeletesChildRow,AlterColumnDropNotNullClearsChild}` (the last two are the mandatory over-fix guard). Design §39. Gates: `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/catalog/`, regress `constraints` 290/15 → 257/13, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS, pgbench smoke via the hook. Two deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19 (recursive pretty-mode deparsing; multi-level `ONLY`-drop grandchild semantics), and the G4 row (0005ae) plus the `\d+`-residue row (0005ag) are flipped to **resolved**.
  - [x] **M0134-0005ag — `pg_get_partition_constraintdef` builtin (bucket D)** — **LANDED 2026-08-19.** The function was seeded in pg_proc (OID 3408, `internal/initdb/pg_proc_seed_data.go:2272`) but had **no executor implementation**, so psql `\d+` failed outright on EVERY partition with `ERROR: function pg_catalog.pg_get_partition_constraintdef does not exist` — regress hunk #13 (31 lines), but the real blast radius was all partition introspection. goopg already stores fully *parsed* partition metadata (`catalog.PartitionBound` `catalog.go:1510-1554`, `PartitionParentOf` `:4563`), so the fix is one dispatch arm in `internal/executor/expr.go` modelled on `pg_get_constraintdef` plus four local render helpers. Two PG facts drove it: `pg_get_partition_constraintdef` returns SQL **NULL, not an error**, for a non-partition or empty qual (`partcache.c:299 get_partition_qual_relid`), and it deparses with `PRETTYFLAG_INDENT` **without** `PRETTYFLAG_PAREN`, so AND/OR add an outer paren pair on top of each self-parenthesizing arm — hence the expected `((a IS NOT NULL) AND (a = 1))`. The research report's claim that PG's `T_NullTest` does *not* self-parenthesize was **wrong** (`ruleutils.c:10224` — it does); the implementation used a local builder rather than `defaultExprToSQL`, so `operators_ddl.go` has **zero diff lines** and the `renderCheckPredicate` / index-predicate siblings are unaffected by construction. Covered: single-level column-key LIST (single value, multi-value `= ANY (ARRAY[…])`, NULL-accepting), single-column RANGE, HASH. DEFAULT partitions, multi-level, multi-column RANGE and expression keys return NULL and are **ledgered** rather than rendered wrong (a plausible-but-wrong qual would be a silent-wrong-answer bug). Design: `docs/design/0134-0005ag-partition-constraintdef.md`. Measurement: constraints **294 lines / 14 hunks -> 290 / 15**; the +1 hunk is a diff **context-split**, verified by the coordinator, not a regression — `Partition constraint: ((a IS NOT NULL) AND (a = 1))` is now a matching CONTEXT line and the residue in that window is two pre-existing unrelated gaps (`CHECK ((a > 0))` vs PG's `CHECK (a > 0)`; missing `(inherited)` tag on inherited NOT NULL), both newly ledgered. Gates: `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/parser/analyzer/`, 7 new `TestPgGetPartitionConstraintdef*` tests FAIL-pre/PASS-post, `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS, pgbench smoke via the hook.

Full bucket map, per-bucket root causes and the ranked next slice live in `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md` (§8 = next slice). Read that doc, not this line, before selecting the next slice. **Re-measured 2026-08-18 after 0005l/0005m: 933 → 865 lines / 29 hunks; after 0005n: 845 lines / 29 hunks.** The CURRENT ranked census is design **§21.1** (supersedes §18.1): the dominant remaining bucket is a 6-sub-bug NOT-NULL/PRIMARY-KEY cluster (18 of 29 hunks, ~600 lines), of which 0005n took the one sub-bug that was line-pinned. **The other five are NOT yet pinned — give each its own research pass before briefing it** (the milestone's twice-paid lesson: size from pinned code, not from the symptom). Largest of them is `get_nnconstraint_info`'s `coninhcount`/`convalidated` drift under multi-level inheritance (~200 lines, silent wrong answer). Never compare to a pre-2026-08-18 number. **After 0005o/0005p: 775 → 763 lines / 30 hunks. After 0005q (2026-08-18, ATTACH/DETACH PARTITION NOT NULL absorption — design §24): 763 → 738 lines / 31 hunks** (the +1 hunk is a pure split of the shrunk hunk, not a new divergence).
  - [x] **M0134-0005n — `ALTER TABLE` never validated a NOT-NULL constraint *name*** — chosen from a fresh measured census at HEAD `9ac2ee3d` (probe `tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005n-census`): **865 lines / 29 hunks**, down from 933/31 after 0005l+0005m, with the dominant remaining bucket a 6-sub-bug NOT-NULL/PRIMARY-KEY cluster (18 of 29 hunks, ~600 lines). This slice took its only line-pinned sub-bug, the one with the largest masking cascade. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** Two silent accepts of statements PG rejects, which in PG are the *same* code path but in goopg are not (goopg's parser keeps an ADD COLUMN's constraint inline on the `ColumnDef` instead of splitting it into a synthesized `AT_AddConstraint` subcommand as `parse_utilcmd.c` does — that asymmetry is why it read as two bugs). **D1:** `ALTER TABLE t ADD CONSTRAINT nn NOT NULL a` on a column that already has a differently-named not-null constraint; the existing duplicate check (`operators_ddl.go:9948-9958`) was keyed on *column name only*, so all three of PG's ordered `AdjustNotNullInheritance` checks were missing, not just the name one. Now raises 55000 with PG-verbatim message/HINT/DETAIL for NO-INHERIT status change, incompatible NOT VALID, and differing explicit name — and the checks run **before** the pre-existing 23502 null scan, matching PG's `AddRelationNewConstraints`-before-`ATRewriteTable` ordering (the fixture caught the wrong order on the first pass). The same-name re-specification stays a successful no-op. **D2:** `ALTER TABLE t ADD COLUMN b INT CONSTRAINT <existing> NOT NULL` silently succeeded, leaving a phantom column that tainted three downstream `\d+` blocks; now 42710 via the existing `fkConstraintNameInUse` predicate, raised before the column is added, plus the `NamedNotNullConstraint` row `execAlterTableAddColumn` never registered (the §18.6 ledger note, confirmed). PG oracle: `postgres/src/backend/catalog/pg_constraint.c:741 AdjustNotNullInheritance` and `heap.c:AddRelationNewConstraints`. Design: `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md` §21. Measured: `constraints` 865 → **845** lines at unchanged 29 hunks (no unmasking). Guards: 6 new tests in `internal/executor/operators_ddl_addnotnull_name_test.go`; `TestAlterTableAddNotNullNamed` had been *asserting the D1 bug* and was corrected (its idempotency guard preserved, not dropped — the 0005m lesson). Ledgered 2026-08-18: the NOT-VALID check still reaches only the ADD-CONSTRAINT arm, not ADD PRIMARY KEY / ADD GENERATED IDENTITY. Gates: `go build ./...`; `go test ./internal/{executor,catalog,parser,optimizer}/`; `go test -run 'TestPort_.*(NotNull|Constraint|Inherit)' ./internal/testport/`; `RALPH_PRECOMMIT_SCOPE=units scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS; `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (**Q12=2, Q13=35** — Rule #1).
  - [x] **M0134-0005o — `ALTER TABLE … ADD PRIMARY KEY` never persisted or cascaded the NOT NULL it synthesized** — rank #2 of the §21.1 NOT-NULL/PRIMARY-KEY cluster; unpinned, so it got its own research pass first (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005o-pknn`) per §21.1's standing rule. That pass **changed the slice**: the symptom read as "goopg does not synthesise NOT NULL for a PK", but goopg *does* (since DU-002 slice 50) — `execAlterTableAddPrimaryKey` already sets `col.NotNull` and registers a `NamedNotNullConstraint`. Two other things were missing. **D1:** the flip never reached the on-disk heap — `pg_attribute` is heap-backed, not virtual, so `\d+`/`pg_dump` (which scan the heap) never saw it; every sibling not-null path already ends with the `catalogHeapSyncAvailable` → `MaterializeWriterXID` → `deleteCatalogRowsForOID` → `syncTableToCatalogHeap` quadruple (`operators_ddl.go:9869-9877`) and the PK path alone omitted it. **D2:** no cascade to inheritance children created *before* the PK was added — M0134-0005i's `cascadeNotNullToChildren` already existed and was simply never called. Guards: 4 in-memory unit tests plus 3 full-server tests querying `pg_attribute.attnotnull` directly, so D1 cannot regress behind a green in-memory test; FAIL-pre/PASS-post verified by stashing `operators_ddl.go` alone. `USING INDEX` (and its `UNIQUE USING INDEX` twin) deliberately excluded and ledgered — those parse to `AlterTableNoOp`, i.e. the whole constraint promotion is unimplemented, a much larger pre-existing debt. Design §22. **Lesson:** "feature missing" and "feature present but not persisted" produce the *same* regress hunk — an in-memory-only catalog mutation is this codebase's recurring silent failure, and a `tbl.AddNotNull` without the heap-resync quadruple beside it is a grep-able bug signature.
  - [x] **M0134-0005p — NOT NULL inheritance bookkeeping (`coninhcount`/`conislocal`/`convalidated`) drifted from PG** — census re-measured at HEAD `09a53e8f`: **775 lines / 29 hunks, an exact match** to the 0005o baseline, so no unmasking and §21.1's ranking held. This slice took the cluster's largest *silent* sub-bug: the fixture's `get_nnconstraint_info` probe (8 call sites) reported wrong counter values under multi-level `INHERITS` and under partitioning — a wrong answer, not an error. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** Root cause is shared: `catalog.Table.AddNotNull` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:253`) is a **pure append that validates nothing**, so each of five hand-written call sites sets `NoInherit`/`NotValid`/`IsLocal`/`InhCount` by hand — no merge-or-create primitive, which is how four independent arithmetic errors accumulated. **A:** `SET NOT NULL`'s already-exists branch fell through to create *and cascade*; PG's existing-constraint branch (`tablecmds.c:7950-8010`) merges in place and returns **without recursing**. **B1:** the cascade hardcoded `notValid=false` rather than propagating the source constraint's. **B2:** the `visited` guard was keyed on child OID alone, so a **diamond**-inherited descendant was counted once instead of once per edge — PG's recursion (`tablecmds.c:8062-8079`) carries **no visited-set at all**, relying on the DAG being acyclic, so re-keying to the (child, immediate-parent) **edge** is the faithful fix. **C:** `PARTITION OF` copied the parent's `NotValid` onto a brand-new *empty* partition, which `heap.c:2385 AddRelationNewConstraints` always cooks `convalidated=t`. Unlike 0005o this is **not** a heap-resync bug — all four sites already run the quadruple; pure bookkeeping arithmetic, a distinct failure mode. Measured **775 → 763 lines**, hunks 29 → 30 (a line-shift split, not a new divergence). **Sizing lesson:** the 5 owning hunks span ~205 lines but are *entangled* with ≥3 independently-rooted bugs (NOTICE suppression, false "inherited more than once", `regclass` ORDER BY), so only ~40-60 lines were ever ours — **hunk span is an upper bound on a sub-bug's size, never an estimate of it**, and the partial shrink was the predicted success criterion. The 0005o baton's #2 filler `pg_get_partition_constraintdef` was **dropped**: zero occurrences in the current fixture, and the `:912` line cited for it is actually this bug's diamond block. `ATTACH PARTITION` absorption (a complete no-op) cut and ledgered — its PG oracle could not be pinned inside `ATExecAttachPartition`, and §21.1's rule is to size from pinned code. Guards: 4 FAIL-pre/PASS-post tests in `internal/testport/notnull_inherit_counters_test.go`. Design doc §23. Four follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18.
  - [x] **M0134-0005q — `ATTACH`/`DETACH PARTITION` never absorbed the partition's NOT NULL constraints** — LANDED 2026-08-18 (`2f1d324d`). New `mergeNotNullOnAttach`/`unmergeNotNullOnDetach` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) mirror PG's `MergeConstraintsIntoExisting` (`tablecmds.c:17638-17817`) and `RemoveInheritance` (`:17950-18144`), wired at all three call sites, each + the child-only heap-resync quadruple. Measured `constraints` **763 → 738 lines**, hunks 30 → 31 (a pure split). Design doc §24; four deferral rows (CHECK-constraint half, `conenforced` branch, the `NoInherit` twin asymmetry).
  - [x] **M0134-0005r — plain `ALTER TABLE … INHERIT / NO INHERIT` never merged NOT NULL** — LANDED 2026-08-18. Upstream these are the *same* function as 0005q's partition half (`CreateInheritance(…, ispartition)` → `MergeConstraintsIntoExisting`, reached from `ATExecAddInherit:17261`); goopg had wired only the partition caller, so the plain-inheritance paths did **no** NOT NULL bookkeeping at all. `mergeNotNullOnAttach` gained an `isPartition bool` gating PG's single `is_partition` delta — `conislocal` is cleared only for a partitioned parent (`tablecmds.c:17771-17777`), so plain INHERITS leaves the child's constraint both local and inherited; `unmergeNotNullOnDetach` needed no change. Wired on **both** execution paths (Rule #2 twins): the immediate cases and the deferred-to-COMMIT `ApplyPendingInheritanceChanges`, with `RegisterInheritanceChild` moved after the merge on each — without that reorder a failed first INHERIT left the edge registered and the correct retry tripped a false 42710. Measured **738 → 731 lines**, hunks 31 → 32; the +1 hunk is an *unmasked* pre-existing `LINE N:`/caret quirk, ledgered. Design doc §25; three deferral rows (no statement-time check on the deferred path; the `ee.Pos` annotation quirk; clusters B/C + the ADD CONSTRAINT cascade).
  - [x] **M0134-0005s — `CREATE TABLE … INHERITS` never marked a locally-sourced NOT NULL as `conislocal`** — LANDED 2026-08-18. Selected from a fresh two-candidate census (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005s-census/`) over the cheaper `ee.Pos` cosmetic, because this is a PG-fidelity bug rather than an error-annotation one. Upstream, `AddRelationNotNullConstraints` (`heap.c:2897`, called from `DefineRelation`) runs two loops: for **every** NOT NULL the child's own body produced — explicit column `NOT NULL`, table-level `CONSTRAINT n NOT NULL c`, **and the PK-implied one** — it stores `islocal=true` unconditionally with the CHILD's `ChooseConstraintName` (`heap.c:3038-3050`); only a column with no local source at all gets the parent's name and `islocal=false` (`heap.c:3057-3120`). goopg decided `isLocal`/`name` earlier, purely from `col.Inherited`, and never revisited them once the local-source list (`entries`) turned out non-empty — so `CREATE TABLE cld2 (PRIMARY KEY (a) DEFERRABLE) INHERITS (parent)` reported the constraint as merely `(inherited)` under the *parent's* name. Fix (design §26.4): capture the child auto-name before the `col.Inherited` branch can overwrite it, then in the `len(entries) > 0` block set `isLocal = true` and fall back to that name when no explicit name was given; `inhCount` untouched, yielding PG's `(local, inherited)`. The Rule #2 twins `mergeNotNullOnAttach`/`unmergeNotNullOnDetach` already had this right from 0005q/0005r and were verified unchanged; the deferred-to-COMMIT INHERIT path shares the same call site and needed nothing. Measured **731 → 707 lines, hunks 32 → 31** — 24 lines, not the predicted 4, because a hunk that goes fully clean takes its context lines with it (per-slice payoff is systematically under-predicted by marker-line counting; §26.8). Design doc §26; two deferral rows (the `NO INHERIT` parent still counted toward the child's `coninhcount` — the ATACC3 case, root cause now pinned at `operators_ddl.go:4012-4023`; and a correction to the `ee.Pos` row, which cited the wrong normaliser and undercounted its own payoff at 6/16/36 lines).
  - [x] **M0134-0005t — a `NO INHERIT` parent NOT NULL was counted toward the child's `coninhcount`** — LANDED 2026-08-18, closing the deferral 0005s opened (the ATACC3 case). Upstream a `NO INHERIT` parent constraint is invisible to inheritance *entirely*: `MergeAttributes` collects the parent's not-nulls via `RelationGetNotNullConstraints(relid, true, include_noinh=false)` (`tablecmds.c:2757`), which skips `connoinherit` rows at the top of its scan (`catalog/pg_constraint.c:834`), and that one filtered list feeds **both** `nncols` (the `attnotnull` request set, `:2759`) and the `nnconstraints` list appended at `:2952`. goopg's two `execCreateTable` parent-scan loops matched on column name alone, so `CREATE TABLE ATACC3 (PRIMARY KEY (a)) INHERITS (ATACC1)` came out `(local, inherited)` where PG emits no tag. Fix is three `if …NoInherit { continue }` guards, 9 lines: the `col.Inherited` loop (`:4013` — one guard kills both the `inhCount` increment and the parent-name assignment), its `!col.Inherited` sibling (`:4043`), and — folding in the Rule-#2 twin deferral from 0005q — `unmergeNotNullOnDetach` (`:13504`), which selected parent constraints without the skip its ATTACH twin `mergeNotNullOnAttach` already had (a no-op today, since the `InhCount > 0` clamp swallowed the decrement, but a live divergence the moment a CHECK or multi-parent extension lands). `:1854-1861`'s `col.NotNull` clearing re-confirmed CORRECT and untouched (§26.9). Measured **707 → 702 lines** at unchanged **31** hunks — the ~4-line estimate met, and unlike 0005s the marker-line prediction held, because this hunk did not go fully clean. Guard `TestPort_NotNullNoInheritParentSkippedOnCreateInherits` asserts all three properties the oracle implies (`coninhcount=0`, `conislocal=true`, name ≠ parent's); FAIL pre / PASS post, verified by stashing the executor edits in-session. Design doc §27; one deferral row (`\d+`'s per-column *Nullable* display shows blank where PG shows `not null` for an inherited + PK-implied column — the one residual ATACC3 line, pre-existing and unchanged by this diff, so a describe-path bug, not this one).
  - [x] **M0134-0005u — goopg stamped a cursor position PG does not set on the NOT-NULL merge errors** — LANDED 2026-08-18, draining the §25.6 quirk ledgered under 0005r/0005s. Both `mergeNotNullOnAttach` call sites wrapped their error return in `if ee, ok := err.(*ExecError); ok && ee.Pos == 0 { ee.Pos = act.Pos() }`, so goopg emitted a `LINE N:`/`^` caret block on the `42P17`/`42804` merge conflicts from `ALTER TABLE … ATTACH PARTITION` and plain `… INHERIT`. PG 18.3 raises both from `MergeConstraintsIntoExisting` (`tablecmds.c:17638-17817`), which calls no `errposition()`, and `constraints.out:1497,1581` carries no caret block. Fix is 6 lines in `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go` (`:8466`, `:9354`); the third call site (`:7416`, the deferred-to-commit path) already discards its error. **The research pass rewrote the slice boundary and corrected the ledger's arithmetic:** 0005s had scoped this as "all ~20 `ee.Pos == 0` sites … worth 36 lines", but only **2** of the 20 are reachable from `constraints.sql` — 15 are lock-acquire/deadlock paths a serial fixture never hits (PG-faithful to remove, zero payoff, and unverified against the `alter-table-*` / `detach-partition-concurrently-*` isolation specs) and 1 belongs to `foreign_key.sql`; both groups re-ledgered. Measured **702/31 → 672 lines / 30 hunks**, against a −6/0 prediction: the 4 directly-removed lines are real and the rest is unified-diff realignment (two hunks merged once the caret lines went), verified non-vacuous by `grep 'conflicts with NOT VALID constraint on child table'` returning zero matches over the new diff and by hand-review of all 30 survivors. Inverse of §27's marker-line rule — a hunk going clean can move far more than its own line count; predict the sign, re-measure the magnitude. Design doc §28; two ledger rows (the 15 lock sites + the FK phase-3 site `:11600`; and the estimate correction itself).
  - [x] **M0134-0005v — `ADD PRIMARY KEY` never verified that the pre-existing NOT NULL is VALID and inheritable** — LANDED 2026-08-18. PG's `verifyNotNullPKCompatible` (`postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:9577-9608`) rejects an `ADD PRIMARY KEY` whose column is already covered by a NOT NULL that is `NO INHERIT` or not validated — such a constraint cannot back a PK — raising `55000` with `cannot create primary key on column "%s"` and a per-arm DETAIL/HINT, and **no** `errposition()`. goopg's `execAlterTableAddPrimaryKey` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) tested only `alreadyHadNotNull := col.NotNull` and `continue`d, never inspecting the `NotNullConstraints` entry's flags. Fix adds `verifyNotNullPKCompatible` plus `verifyNotNullPKCompatibleChildren` (PG's `!recurse` branch, `:9532-9557`, naming the CHILD in the DETAIL as the oracle does) and threads `only` from the single call site (`:8096`); the block runs before index creation and the null scan, matching PG's `ATPrepAddPrimaryKey`-before-`ATExecAddIndex` order. **Measured 672 → 647 lines / 30 hunks** (predicted ~662). **The selection is the transferable lesson:** the §28 baton's top two ranked candidates — `ATExecValidateConstraint` descendant recursion and the CHECK half of `MergeConstraintsIntoExisting` — are real, wholly-unimplemented divergences that the `constraints.sql` fixture **never reaches** (all five `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` sites have zero descendants at that moment, `:904` inverting because `:901`'s `INHERIT` makes `notnull_chld0` the parent; all five `INHERIT` sites carry NOT NULL only, never a shared-name CHECK). A reachability pass retired both and surfaced this in-diff 4th candidate at 2.5x the payoff of the surviving ranked #3. Second consecutive loop where reachability, not a site census, picked the slice — **a carried ranking is a hypothesis, not a queue**. Second lesson: where the brief's file/line pointer and the design section's fixture list disagreed, the fixture list was the real scope (the narrow reading would have closed 1 of 3 sites). Design doc §29; four ledger rows (the child-has-no-NOT-NULL arm; the two unreachable candidates; the identity-column `NOT VALID` check at `constraints.sql:838`; the latent plain-INHERITS drop-cascade gap). Gates: `go build ./...`; `go test ./internal/executor/`; the 5 new guard tests in `operators_ddl_addpk_notnull_pkcompat_test.go` (FAIL-pre/PASS-post); `TestPort_.*(NotNull|Constraint|Inherit|Partition)` (54 subtests, 75.1s); `scripts/pg-regress-runner.sh constraints` 647/30; UNITS PASS (7m48s, `internal/initdb`+`cmd/goopg` cold-cache); `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (**Q12=2, Q13=35**, Rule #1); pgbench smoke via hook.
