  - [x] **M0134-0005x — `ADD CONSTRAINT … {PRIMARY KEY|UNIQUE} USING INDEX` was a silent no-op** — LANDED 2026-08-19 (`2ee3a987`). Parser discarded the index name; new `adoptExistingIndexAsConstraint` + `finishPrimaryKeyConstraint`. Design §31; 601→555 lines / 28→26 hunks.
  - [x] **M0134-0005y — `CREATE TABLE` never re-synced pg_attribute after its constraint arms flipped NOT NULL** — LANDED 2026-08-19 (`a10792d9`). pg_class is virtual but pg_attribute is heap-backed, so `\d+`/pg_dump read a stale `attnotnull`; `notNullHeapDirty` flag at all four mutation sites + mandatory delete-old-then-resync. Design §32; 555→516 lines / 26→23 hunks.
  - [x] **M0134-0005z — plain `INHERITS` never copied the parent's CHECK constraints, so they were NEVER ENFORCED on child INSERT (a SILENT INTEGRITY GAP, same class as 0005e/0005h/0005l)** — LANDED 2026-08-19. Enforcement (`operators_fk.go:1700 checkConstraints`) was already correct and data-driven; `execCreateTable`'s `s.Inherits` loop simply never walked `parent.NamedChecks`, while `execCreatePartitionChild` (`PARTITION OF`) had always done it right. Fix folds each parent's CHECKs via `AddCheckInherited` (skip `NO INHERIT`, dedup across parents), plus the inseparable second bug: column-level CHECK auto-naming used the syntactically-attached column instead of PG's distinct-referenced-column rule (`heap.c:ChooseConstraintName`) — goopg already had `autoCheckName`, it just wasn't called. Exposed two latent flags that had never mattered because nothing ever inherited a CHECK: `CHECK … NO INHERIT` was parsed but persisted as `false`, and `NotEnforced` was not carried onto inherited entries. Design §33; **516→465 lines / 23→22 hunks**. Two ledger rows: the `ALTER TABLE … INHERIT` CHECK-merge + `ADD CONSTRAINT CHECK` inheritance-cascade gaps (same integrity hole on the post-creation paths, zero fixture reachability), and `AddCheckInherited` not carrying `NotEnforced`/`NotValid` to its two partition call sites.
  - [x] **M0134-0005aa — a CHECK on `tableoid` was never ENFORCED, and a CHECK on any other system column was never REJECTED (two halves of one upstream rule; the first is a SILENT INTEGRITY GAP, same class as 0005e/0005h/0005z)** — LANDED 2026-08-19. Selected from a fresh census (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005aa-census/`) that re-confirmed the **465 lines / 22 hunks** baseline unchanged by 0005y/0005z. PG's rule is one site: `postgres/src/backend/parser/parse_relation.c:707-713` (`scanNSItemForColumn`, `EXPR_KIND_CHECK_CONSTRAINT`) — no system column but `tableoid`, else 42703 with a cursor position. (A) `checkConstraints` (`internal/executor/operators_fk.go:1700`) re-plans each CHECK against a synthetic `VALUES` alias built only from real columns, so `tableoid` failed to plan and the error was swallowed by `if err != nil { continue }` — the constraint was skipped and the row PASSED. Fixed by binding `tableoid` as `<tbl.OID>::oid`. **(A2, the durable half)** every parse/plan/build/open/next failure in that loop now returns `XX000` naming constraint/relation/stage instead of silently passing; only the three legitimate no-ops (empty expr, `NOT ENFORCED`, NULL result) still `continue`. (B) new `validateCheckExprSystemColumns` (`operators_ddl.go`, reusing `collectCheckExprColumns`) rejects `ctid`/`xmin`/`xmax`/`cmin`/`cmax` at **both** `execCreateTable` and `ALTER TABLE … ADD CONSTRAINT … CHECK` (Rule-#2 sibling pair), running before `Catalog.CreateTable` so a rejection leaves no half-created relation. Measured **465 → 460 lines**, hunks 22 → 23 (context splitting, not new divergence). Guards: `TestCheckConstraintTableoidEnforcedOnInsert`, `…SystemColumnRejectedAtCreateTable`, `…SystemColumnRejectedAtAlterTable`, `…TableoidAcceptedAtAlterTable` (the last guards against over-rejection). Design §34. **Three deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19**: PG's cursor position on that error (parser tracks no offset for CHECK bodies); the GENERATED-column half of the same rule (goopg validates generated-column exprs not at all); and the chained-cast output-column LABEL bug (`targetMeta`, `internal/optimizer/planner.go:11423-11449` — general, not tableoid-specific).
  - [x] **M0134-0005ab — plain `INHERITS` never merged a redeclared column, so it silently created that column TWICE (and double-fired the multi-parent NOTICE)** — LANDED 2026-08-19 at the **460 lines / 23 hunks** baseline left by 0005aa. PG collects columns in two ordered passes (`postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c`, `MergeAttributes`): the `INHERITS` parent scan first, then the child's own declared columns; a child column colliding by name with an inherited one is **merged into the existing entry** by `MergeChildAttribute` (`tablecmds.c:3259`) with `NOTICE: merging column "%s" with inherited definition`, while `MergeInheritedAttribute` (`tablecmds.c:3430`) emits `merging multiple inherited definitions of column "%s"` N−1 times across N parents. `transformTableLikeClause` (`parse_utilcmd.c`) emits NONE of these — LIKE columns are just more entries the same merge logic sees, which is why ONE shared path is the faithful shape. goopg had the merge **only** inside the `@@LIKE:` branch of `execCreateTable` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`); the plain explicit-column path (`addCol`) never consulted `inheritedColNames` and unconditionally appended, with no de-duplication anywhere before `catalog.InMemory.CreateTable` — a textbook Hard-won-Rule-#2 miss (two siblings of one rule, one silently unimplemented). Three symptoms, one cause: (1) no NOTICE; (2) a **genuinely duplicated column** — verified live pre-fix, `c (a int, b int) INHERITS (p)` produced **four** `pg_attribute` rows; (3) a DOUBLE multi-parent NOTICE on the 3-level `notnull_inhparent`/`notnull_inhchild`/`notnull_inhgrand` chain — that site was **not itself buggy**, it iterated a middle table whose own `Columns` already carried `i` twice, so fixing `addCol` corrected it with ZERO changes there (the brief forbade touching it in round 1 for exactly this reason). Fix: a shared `mergeExplicitColumnIntoInherited` helper owning the whole rule (collision lookup, the 42701 "specified more than once" error for a non-inherited collision, storage/type-conflict checks, the NOT-NULL OR-in, the NOTICE), called by BOTH callers with the LIKE branch's inline copy DELETED so the siblings cannot drift again. Constraint identity needed a provenance signal `col.Inherited` cannot carry (it is false whenever the child redeclares at all): a new `explicitNotNullLocal` derived from `NotNullExplicit` separates "child retyped only" (merge — parent's `conname`, `conislocal=false`, `coninhcount=1`) from "child wrote `NOT NULL` itself" (`child2_t` — stays local). The pre-existing `child2_t` assertion was kept **untouched as the over-merge guard**. Measured **460 → 406 lines, 23 → 21 hunks**. Guards (FAIL-pre/PASS-post): `TestInheritsMergesRedeclaredColumnCount`, `TestInheritsThreeLevelChainMergeNoticeFiresOnce`, `TestInheritsRedeclaredColumnKeepsInheritedNotNullIdentity`. Design §35. **Two deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19**: the `SET NOT NULL` cascade not propagating `convalidated=true` to an already-merged descendant constraint; and PG's `moving and merging column` / ALTER-TABLE-ADD-COLUMN `merging definition of column … for child` variants.
  - [x] **M0134-0005ac — the NOT-NULL / PRIMARY-KEY verification scan did not cover the rows it must: it either never ran (VALIDATE CONSTRAINT) or scanned storage that is empty by construction (a partitioned parent)** — LANDED 2026-08-19 at the **406 lines / 21 hunks** baseline left by 0005ab. Chosen from a fresh measured census at HEAD `3083cd1c` (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005ac-census/`, 21 hunks attributed to 12 buckets); this slice took the two buckets that are one root cause with four symptoms. **B — no scan at all:** `ALTER TABLE … VALIDATE CONSTRAINT <nn>` flipped `NotValid = false` with zero row inspection, guarded by an in-code comment claiming PG never scans for `CONSTR_NOTNULL`. That comment is **factually wrong** and conflates two PG sites: `postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:9956` excludes `CONSTR_NOTNULL` from the generic *ADD*-constraint Phase-3 queue, but `ATExecValidateConstraint`'s notnull branch (`tablecmds.c:13291-13295`) independently sets `tab->verify_new_notnull = true`, which DOES trigger a full-table scan. **C — the scan read the wrong storage:** `forEachLiveRow` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) scanned exactly `o.ctx.Catalog.RelFileNode(tbl)`, i.e. the target's OWN heap. A partitioned parent has no own storage, so `SET NOT NULL` and `ADD PRIMARY KEY` on such a parent scanned zero rows and wrongly succeeded over NULL-bearing partitions. Fix: `forEachLiveRow` now recurses the partition/inheritance tree via the `allDescendants` + `snapDetachEpoch` helpers `validateFKConstraintExistingRows` already used, delegating to a new `forEachLiveRowRel(targetTbl, leafTbl, fn)` that scans the leaf's heap, decodes with the leaf's `Columns`, and remaps **by name** into a parent-shaped buffer (a partition's physical column order may differ) — mirroring `validateFKConstraintExistingRowsRel`. The VALIDATE branch gained a real 23502 scan gated on `NotValid`. **One signature change, forced by the oracle:** the callback became `func(row Row, relName string) error` at all 5 call sites, because PG's phase-3 error names the relation actually holding the violating row, not the ALTER TABLE target — `constraints.out:1254` says `cnn_part1` (not `cnn2_parted`) and `:1561` says `notnull_tbl1_3` (not `notnull_tbl1`); the first pass named the target and the regress runner caught it. Sibling audit (Rule #2) over all 5 callers: SET NOT NULL / ADD NOT NULL / ADD PRIMARY KEY recurse in PG too (correct); the new VALIDATE branch is non-recursive in PG as well (correct, it targets exactly the named relation); `validateCheckConstraintRows` now also sees descendant rows — **more** PG-correct, but inert today because goopg's ADD CHECK does not cascade CHECK *registration* to partitions (ledgered 2026-08-19). Measured **406 → 381 lines, 21 → 19 hunks** (−25 lines, −2 hunks): hunks 13, 17, 18a and 18b closed. One new `@@` header opened, and it is an **unmasking, not a regression** — `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT nn3` on a partition used to succeed silently, which accidentally left `nn3` in the state PG's real cascade produces; now that it correctly ERRORs, the already-ledgered `SET NOT NULL` descendant-`convalidated` cascade gap (0005ab's ledger row, census standing candidate #2 / hunk 19) surfaces at a second script location. Guards (FAIL-pre/PASS-post, `internal/executor/operators_ddl_notnull_partition_scan_test.go`): `TestAlterTableValidateConstraintNotNullScansExistingRows`, `TestAlterTableValidateConstraintNotNullOnPartitionScans`, `TestAlterTableSetNotNullOnPartitionedParentScansPartitions`, `TestAlterTableAddPrimaryKeyOnPartitionedParentScansPartitions`, plus the over-fix guard `TestAlterTableSetNotNullAndValidateStillSucceedWithoutNulls` (a plain table with no NULLs must still succeed).
  - [x] **M0134-0005ad — two NOT-NULL constraint checks the *direct* path made and the *derived* path skipped** — bucket E of the 19-hunk census (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005ac-census/report.md`). **LANDED 2026-08-19.** One shape, two sites, and both are Hard-won Rule #2 in its least obvious form: the sibling pair is not encode/decode but **direct target vs recursion target**, and **first column vs later column of the same statement**. **E1:** `cascadeNotNullToChildrenAt` bumped a child's `InhCount` with no re-check, so a NO-INHERIT conflict on a *descendant* went unreported even though `AlterTableAddNotNull` raised exactly that 55000 for the ALTER target itself; PG has no asymmetry because the check lives INSIDE the recursion (`tablecmds.c:10012-10043` → `heap.c:2609-2662` → `AdjustNotNullInheritance`, `pg_constraint.c:741-767`). The error names the **child**, per the same oracle rule §36.4 established. The hunk's second `+ERROR` ("cannot drop inherited constraint") needed no separate fix — the false merge made a local constraint look inherited. **E2:** two columns of one `CREATE TABLE` naming the same NOT NULL constraint now give 42710 via the existing `fkConstraintNameInUse` helper. E2 also exposed a trap worth the ledger row it got: `Catalog.CreateTable` runs BEFORE constraint processing and is not transactional, so the naive rejection left a phantom relation that made the script's next `CREATE TABLE` fail 42P07 — the error path must compensate with an explicit `DropTable`, and every other post-`CreateTable` error return in `execCreateTable` still leaks. Measured **381 → 360 lines, 19 → 18 hunks**, no new `@@` header. Design: `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md` §37. Guards `TestAlterTableAddNotNullCascadeNoInheritMismatchRejected` / `TestCreateTableDuplicateExplicitNotNullNameRejected` (FAIL-pre/PASS-post) in `internal/executor/operators_ddl_notnull_cascade_dup_test.go`. Deferred: the CREATE-time (`mergeNotNullEntries`) twin of E1, and the phantom-relation class.
  - [x] **M0134-0005ae — two constraint properties lost while *cloning* a constraint from another relation** — bucket G of the census (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005ae-research/report.md`), the two lowest-risk line-pinned items of four; the other two (G2 sequence-DEFAULT schema qualification, G4 `ALTER TABLE ONLY` drop-orphaning) are deliberately OUT of this slice and ledgered. **LANDED 2026-08-19.** One shape, two unrelated clone paths: goopg copies a constraint onto a *different* relation and drops a property PG carries across. **G3** `CREATE TABLE … (LIKE t INCLUDING INDEXES)` copied only the source PK's `idx.Columns` into `s.PrimaryKey` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go:2142`), never `s.PrimaryKeyDeferrable`/`s.PrimaryKeyInitiallyDeferred`, so the unconditional downstream reader at `operators_ddl.go:3708-3711` always built a non-deferrable clone — while the *directly-declared* PK path (`:3701-3731`) and the *named-constraint* PK path (`:3665-3679`) both threaded deferrability correctly. Third sibling now learns the same trick; PG oracle `parse_utilcmd.c:1804-1805` (`generateClonedIndexStmt`) copies `condeferrable`/`condeferred` when cloning ANY index. **G1** `ALTER TABLE … ATTACH PARTITION` assigned the parent FK's name to the child verbatim (`internal/executor/operators_fk.go:581`) with no collision check, so a child already owning a same-named CHECK kept the ambiguous name; PG renames to `<name>_1` via `ChooseConstraintName` (`pg_constraint.c:513`) from `addFkConstraint` (`tablecmds.c:10748-10760`). Fixed with a NEW additive helper `constraintNameTaken(ctx, tbl, name)` beside `checkNameTaken` — the existing helper scanned only `tbl.NamedChecks`, but PG's `pg_constraint` name namespace is per-relation across CHECK + FK + PK/unique/exclusion, which is exactly why this collision (FK clone vs existing CHECK) existed at all; `checkNameTaken`'s own callers are untouched. **Measurement:** constraints **360 → 322 lines, 18 → 16 hunks**, no new `@@` — the line drop (38) far exceeded the 7 the two hunks contained, because both bugs were masking downstream `\d+` blocks. Never compare to a pre-2026-08-19 number. Guards `TestLikeIncludingIndexesCopiesPKDeferrable` and `TestAttachPartitionFKNameCollisionRenamed`, both FAIL-pre/PASS-post.
  - [x] **M0134-0005af — the two *located* exclusion-constraint gaps: a build that never scanned, and an arbiter check that never asked** — bucket F of the census, re-pinned at the **322 lines / 16 hunks** baseline left by 0005ae (`tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005af-research/report.md`). **LANDED 2026-08-19.** **F9 (a SILENT INTEGRITY GAP, same class as 0005aa/0005ac):** `ALTER TABLE … ADD EXCLUDE (col WITH =)` succeeded over data that violates the constraint it was creating. goopg backs a btree-equality exclusion with a real btree so `checkExclusionConstraintsForInsert` can probe it later, and `execAlterTableAddExclude` passed `unique=false` to `createBTreeIndex` — but `unique` was the ONLY gate on `collectBTreeEntries`' duplicate scan, the sole place in the entire build path that ever inspects existing rows. The naive fix is wrong twice: `idx.Unique` is read elsewhere and an exclusion constraint is not a unique constraint, and the CREATE-TABLE-time `EXCLUDE (col WITH =)` branch passes the identical `unique=false` (harmless there only because CREATE TABLE has no pre-existing rows) — flipping one site would leave two producers of one constraint shape building catalog-divergent indexes, Rule #2 in its 'two producers of one artifact' form. Landed fix **decouples the two meanings**: a new `CheckDup` field on the variadic `btreeIndexProps` drives the scan while `unique` still solely drives `idx.Unique`; both sibling call sites pass the same props; all other callers are byte-identical (`checkDup = unique`). `IsExclusion`/`ExclusionOp` moved onto the same struct for an ordering reason: `execAlterTableAddExclude` already set `idx.IsExclusion`, but *after* `LookupIndex` — i.e. after the build had already had to choose its error shape. PG oracle `postgres/src/backend/executor/execIndexing.c:893-918` (`check_exclusion_or_unique_constraint`, `newIndex` branch): **23P01** `could not create exclusion constraint "%s"` with a **two-sided** `Key %s conflicts with key %s.`, vs the unique path's one-sided 23505 `… is duplicated.` — the duplicate block now branches on `idx.IsExclusion`; the 23505 branch is untouched. **F8:** `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT <exclusion constraint>` returned `42P10 … is not a unique constraint` — two sibling sites (`analyzeOnConflict`, `internal/parser/analyzer/analyzer.go`; and its own defensive re-check `resolveArbiterIndex`, `internal/optimizer/planner.go`) gated arbiter validity on `idx.Unique` alone, which is false for EVERY exclusion constraint, deferred or not — a broader bug than the regress case shows. PG (`execIndexing.c:592-610`, `ExecCheckIndexConstraints`) does two distinct things goopg conflated: `!ii_Unique && !ii_ExclusionOps` skips a non-candidate, then `!indimmediate` raises **55000** `ON CONFLICT does not support deferrable unique constraints/exclusion constraints as arbiters`. Both goopg sites now read `!idx.Unique && !idx.IsExclusion` (unchanged 42P10) followed by `idx.InitiallyDeferred` → 55000. The gate is `InitiallyDeferred`, **not** `Deferrable`: PG keys on `indimmediate`, false only for `INITIALLY DEFERRED`; a plain `DEFERRABLE` constraint is a valid arbiter. The new error carries `Pos: 0` — PG raises it at execution time with no `errposition()`, so goopg may hoist the check (the property is catalog-static) but must not hoist a cursor position with it. **Measured 322 → 294 lines, 16 → 14 hunks**, both targeted hunks closed, `deferred_excl` now byte-identical to PG, **no new `@@`**. Guards (FAIL-pre/PASS-post, both directions verified): `TestAlterTableAddExcludeRejectsExistingConflict`, `TestAlterTableAddExcludeSucceedsOverCleanData` (over-rejection guard — clean data must still succeed), `TestAnalyzeOnConflict{AcceptsExclusionConstraintArbiter,RejectsDeferrableExclusionArbiter,RejectsDeferrableUniqueArbiter}`, `TestResolveArbiterIndex{AcceptsExclusionConstraintArbiter,RejectsDeferrableExclusionArbiter}` — the planner pair calls `resolveArbiterIndex` directly because `Plan()` runs `analyzer.Analyze` first and the analyzer's check masks the planner's (a test-topology trap for the next toucher). Design §38. **Two deferrals ledgered 2026-08-19**: **F7** — the census's 'missing circle gist opclass, catalog seed data' framing is WRONG (`gist/circle_ops` is seeded correctly); the real blocker is a hardcoded box-only allow-list in `createExclusionIndexStub` plus `checkGistOverlapExclusion`'s single-column-box-only support, i.e. a multi-piece feature whose **partial** implementation would be worse than none (catalogued-but-unenforced exclusion constraints — the exact class F9 just closed); and the unexamined general question of which PG execution-time diagnostics goopg may hoist to analyze time and how their cursor positions must be suppressed.
  - [x] **M0134-0005a — PREPARE/EXECUTE declared parameter types are never coerced** — `internal/postmaster/dispatch.go` uses a prepared statement's declared `prepDef.paramTypes` only for an arity check and a coarse `execParamTypeIncompatible` gate, never as an actual coercion of the bound value, so `PREPARE p(regclass) AS SELECT pg_typeof($1)` + `EXECUTE p('pg_class')` reports `unknown`. Generic (not reg\*-specific); likely mis-binds numeric/interval/date too. **This is the direct blocker for the `EXECUTE get_nnconstraint_info(…)` → `(0 rows)` hunks and the next slice for M0134-0005** — the `reg*[]` cast it must land on is now fixed. PG oracle: `postgres/src/backend/commands/prepare.c:EvaluateParams` (`coerce_to_target_type`). Design: `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md` §7 root cause B; ledgered 2026-08-18. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** `executor.CoerceParamToDeclaredType` (`internal/executor/expr.go`, beside `evalCast`) normalises the declared spelling (quotes stripped, lower-cased, typmod parenthesis dropped, trailing `[]` kept) and delegates to `evalCast`; **both** dispatch sites call it — the `*parser.ExecuteStmt` branch and the `CREATE TABLE … AS EXECUTE` branch of `dispatchSimpleQueryViaExecutor` (a Rule-#2 sibling pair, blind on both sides). Cast failures now surface the cast's own SQLSTATE (42P01) instead of a silent wrong answer. Scalar `reg*` targets resolve inside the wrapper via `regIdentifierInput`, deliberately NOT by widening `evalCast`'s `regclass` case (tried, reverted — regressed `TestRegCastToStringRendersName`). Measured: `constraints` 1411 → **1376** lines (hunks 33 → 34 — unmasking split), `get_nnconstraint_info` mentions 15 → 14, all survivors now mere unified-diff context lines. Guards: `TestExecuteCoercesRegclassArrayParam`, `TestExecuteCoercesScalarRegclassParam`, `TestExecuteRegclassParamNoSuchRelationErrors`, `TestCreateTableAsExecuteCoercesParam` (`internal/postmaster/execute_param_coercion_test.go`). Design: §8 of the same doc. Two follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18 (`pg_typeof($1)` plan-time fold; the five unresolved `reg*` parameter types).
  - [x] **M0134-0005b — Bucket 4: `DEFERRABLE` unique constraints had no statement-end check tier** — goopg had TWO tiers where PG has THREE. PG sets `pg_index.indimmediate = false` for **every** deferrable index regardless of its INITIALLY mode (`postgres/src/backend/catalog/index.c:2080-2082`), so the per-row insert takes `UNIQUE_CHECK_PARTIAL` (`execIndexing.c:ExecInsertIndexTuples`, never errors) and the recheck fires from the after-trigger queue (`unique_key_recheck`); only the recheck **timing** differs by INITIALLY mode. goopg's `uniqueCheckDeferred` collapsed both deferrable tiers into one boolean gated on "deferred to commit" **and** on `InExplicitTransaction()`, so plain `DEFERRABLE` still blocked on the first transient duplicate — `UPDATE unique_tbl SET i = i + 1;` failed even inside `BEGIN`. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** The milestone-sized machinery already existed (M0119-0004's `deferred_unique.go` commit-tier queue) — the research pass refuted the §2 "milestone" sizing and it was one slice. `uniqueCheckDeferred` now answers only "needs a partial check?" (`idx != nil && idx.Deferrable`, explicit-txn gate removed); new companion `uniqueCheckDeferToCommit` holds the old resolver; `queueDeferredUniqueCheck` **and** its NND twin stamp a `DeferToCommit` tier tag; new `RunStmtEndDeferredUniqueChecks` drains only the non-commit tier. Drain hooks added at BOTH dispatch twins — and they were **not** symmetric: the extended-protocol out-of-block `Execute` path commits via `ectx.CommitTransaction` (`internal/executor/context.go:1030`), a bare `TxnMgr.Commit` that ran **no** deferred-check drain at all. Guards: 5 tests in `internal/testport/deferred_unique_stmt_end_e2e_test.go` (3 FAIL-pre/PASS-post). Measured: `constraints` 1376 → **1299** lines (−77), hunks 34 → 36 (unmasking split). Design: §10 of the same doc. Three follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18.
  - [x] **M0134-0005c — DDL bulk scans got tuple liveness BACKWARDS under abort (EXPOSED by 0005b; capped the rest of Bucket 4)** — `ALTER TABLE unique_tbl ADD CONSTRAINT unique_tbl_i_key UNIQUE (i) DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` failed `could not create unique index` / `DETAIL: Key (i)=(1) is duplicated` while the preceding `SELECT *` matched PG byte-for-byte. **The §10.4 hypothesis ("the build scan counts dead row versions") was REFUTED by direct probe** — a plain committed `UPDATE` leaving superseded versions behind provokes nothing. The real trigger is the **preceding `BEGIN; UPDATE …; ROLLBACK;`** block. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** Three DDL bulk-scan functions in `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go` decided liveness by a purely structural test (`Xmin==Invalid ⇒ dead`, `Xmax!=Invalid ⇒ dead`) that never consulted commit/abort status, so after an aborted UPDATE they got liveness wrong **in both directions**: the real row (non-invalid but *aborted* xmax) was dropped, and the aborted UPDATE's phantom `i=1` version (*aborted* xmin) was kept, colliding with the legitimate `i=1` produced later. Nothing prunes the phantom, so the page stayed poisoned for every later `CREATE INDEX`/`ADD CONSTRAINT`/FK-validate. Fix: `collectBTreeEntries` (~:11890), `forEachLiveRow` (~:11261) and `validateFKConstraintExistingRows` (~:11396) now call the **pre-existing** abort-aware helper `isLiveForUniqueCheck` (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go:8767-8832`, already used at 6+ INSERT/UPDATE-time sites, simply never wired into the DDL scans); no new predicate, no parameter threading (`o.ctx` was already in scope at all three). Rule #2: `ADD CONSTRAINT … UNIQUE` and `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX` are **not** two paths — both route through `bulkBuildBTreeFull` → `collectBTreeEntries` — and that was measured, not assumed. PG oracle: `postgres/src/backend/access/heap/heapam_visibility.c:1205` `HeapTupleSatisfiesVacuumHorizon` (aborted xmin ⇒ `HEAPTUPLE_DEAD`), consumed by `heapam_handler.c:1415-1613` `heapam_index_build_range_scan`. Guards: 3 tests in `internal/testport/ddl_scan_abort_liveness_test.go`, all FAIL-pre/PASS-post — including the **inverse** direction (a rolled-back DELETE's aborted xmax must not hide a live duplicate), whose pre-fix behaviour was a *silent false success*, not an error. Measured: `constraints` 1299 → **1251** lines (−48), hunks 36 → **35**, and the `could not create unique index`/`is duplicated` error class is gone from the diff entirely. Design: §10.4/§10.5 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. Two follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18 (dead-code `backfillBTree` retains the inverted check; aborted-xmin prune behaviour unestablished).
  - [x] **M0134-0005d — `[NOT] ENFORCED` clause fidelity (design-doc research slices 3 and 4, landed together)** — two line-disjoint parts, both pinned by an empirical probe before briefing. **(A) grammar must ACCEPT `[NOT] ENFORCED` and then reject it semantically.** PG treats `ENFORCED` as an ordinary `ConstraintAttributeElem` (`gram.y:4135-4150`, `n->location = @1`) and rejects it in `parse_utilcmd.c:3991-4021` `transformConstraintAttrs` for UNIQUE/PRIMARY KEY/EXCLUSION with `misplaced [NOT] ENFORCED clause`, SQLSTATE **42601**, **with** a caret (under `ENFORCED` for the bare form, under `NOT` for the pair — counted against `postgres/src/test/regress/expected/constraints.out:740-746`, not assumed). goopg's failure was a token-stream accident: `parseConstraintDeferrable` (`internal/parser/ddl.go:2786`) unconditionally swallows a leading `NOT` while probing for `NOT DEFERRABLE`, so `NOT ENFORCED` lost its `NOT` and left `ENFORCED` dangling, while bare `ENFORCED` was never inspected. Fix = new `rejectMisplacedEnforced` reusing the **pre-existing** `isEnforcedAttr` (`ddl.go:2818`, which requires an `ENFORCED` peek so it never matches `NOT NULL`/`NOT VALID`/`NOT DEFERRABLE`) and the `SyntaxError{Pos, Message, Raw:true}` template already used by `rejectDuplicateEnforced`; called **before** `parseConstraintDeferrable` at all four column-level sites (bare `UNIQUE`, bare `PRIMARY KEY`, and both named-inline `CONSTRAINT <c> …` spellings) — the ordering **is** the fix. Table-level `UNIQUE (i) ENFORCED` deliberately untouched: PG routes it through a different production (`processCASbits`) with a different message and SQLSTATE (`"UNIQUE constraints cannot be marked ENFORCED"`, 0A000), so reusing the column-level text would have been a new divergence; ledgered. **(B) `ALTER CONSTRAINT … [NOT] ENFORCED` carried a position PG does not.** Message + SQLSTATE (42809) already matched byte-for-byte; only a spurious `LINE`/caret differed, from `Pos: act.Pos()` on two `ExecError`s in `execAlterTableAlterConstraint` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go` ~:11061, ~:11091). PG's `tablecmds.c:12254-12258` `ereport` has no `parser_errposition`; dropping the field suffices (`Pos == 0` ⇒ no wire `'P'` field). The four sibling sites were left alone **on purpose** — one of them (`constraints cannot be altered to be NOT VALID`) currently matches PG *with* a caret, so a blanket sweep would regress it; ledgered. Measured: `constraints` 1251 → **1232** lines (−19), hunks 35 → **35**, and every `+`/`-` ENFORCED line is gone (only three context lines remain). Guards: `TestParseColumnConstraintMisplacedEnforced` (8 cases + a DEFERRABLE-trailer regression block), a `Pos == 0` assertion in `TestFKAlterConstraint/NonFKConstraintRejected`, and `internal/postmaster/exec_error_position_test.go`; both production fixes FAIL-pre/PASS-post by stashing the single changed file. Design: §11 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. Two follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18.
  - [x] **M0134-0005e — the COMMIT-tier deferred UNIQUE recheck was blind to HOT chains (a SILENT integrity gap — goopg committed a transaction PG rejects)** — unlike every earlier bucket in this case the divergence was not a wrong message: `BEGIN; INSERT (dup key); UPDATE <non-key col of the new row>; COMMIT;` **succeeded** on goopg, leaving a real duplicate on disk, where PG 18.3 raises `23505 duplicate key value violates unique constraint` at COMMIT and rolls back. Upstream case: `postgres/src/test/regress/sql/constraints.sql:510-517`, whose own comment names the scenario ("test a HOT update that invalidates the conflicting tuple. the trigger should still fire and catch the violation"). **LANDED 2026-08-18.** Root cause, pinned by a live two-engine probe before briefing: `recheckDeferredUniqueKey` (`internal/executor/deferred_unique.go`) scanned the b-tree and fetched each returned `ItemPointer`'s tuple **at exactly that pointer** (`storage.PageGetHeapTuple`), never following `t_ctid`. A non-key `UPDATE` takes the HOT path (`tryApplyHOTUpdate`, `operators_storage.go:3896-4136`) which — correctly, matching PG — does **no** index maintenance, so the candidate's only b-tree entry pointed at a slot `isLiveForUniqueCheck` *correctly* called dead (`xmax == selfXID`, `operators_storage.go:8805-8808`) while the live successor sat one hop away, unvisited: live count 1 instead of 2, check passes, silent commit. Everything else in the chain was verified-not-assumed to be already right — the UPDATE is deliberately not re-enqueued (`indexKeyColumnsChanged`, `operators_storage.go:8312`, as PG does) and the commit drain does run on both commit paths (`internal/postmaster/txn_verb.go:333`, `internal/executor/operators_tx.go:140`). Fix: new `resolveDeferredUniqueChainTail` resolves each candidate pointer to its chain tail before the liveness test, reusing the **pre-existing** `isChainTailCTID` + `maxCTIDChainWalk` primitives (no new predicate; `isLiveForUniqueCheck` stays the sole liveness judge), and `seen` now de-duplicates by the **resolved tail** pointer so converging chains are not double-counted. **Design deviation, and it is the PG-faithful one:** the walk follows a `t_ctid` hop only while the predecessor carries `HeapHotUpdated`, NOT unconditionally — a naive full-chain follow over-counted a *resolved* duplicate as live (HOT update followed by a key-changing non-HOT update) and was caught by this slice's own negative guard. That matches PG's real `unique_key_recheck`, which lives in `postgres/src/backend/commands/constraint.c:39-121` (**not** `trigger.c` — the first citation was off by file) and fetches only through `heap_hot_search_buffer`, explicitly relying on "the columns of interest to us are the same" (`constraint.c:98`) — a guarantee that holds across HOT hops only. Rule #2 sibling check came back **negative** and was confirmed by reading, not grepping: `recheckDeferredNNDUniqueKey` is heap-scan based (`scanNNDLiveMatches`) and resolves no stored index pointers. Not a protocol divergence either — both dispatch twins reach the same shared drain, so one fix covers both. Guards: `TestPort_DeferredUniqueCommitCatchesHOTUpdatedDuplicate` (FAIL-pre `got: <nil>`, no 23505 / PASS-post, proven by stashing only the changed production file) **and** a negative companion `TestPort_DeferredUniqueCommitAllowsHOTResolvedDuplicate` — the second guard is what caught the over-counting deviation above, and is the reason a one-directional guard was not enough. Measured: `constraints` 1232 → **1181** lines (−51), hunks 35 → **33**, and the whole `unique_tbl` block is **gone** from the diff (`grep unique_tbl` returns zero matches). Design: §12 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. Two follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18.
  - [x] **M0134-0005f — per-partition index clones dropped the parent's constraint attributes (`Deferrable`/`InitiallyDeferred`/`IsConstraint`)** — upstream case `postgres/src/test/regress/sql/constraints.sql:432-445` (`parted_uniq_tbl`), two symptoms from ONE cause, both pinned by a live two-engine probe before briefing. (1) `pg_constraint` listed only the parent's row where PG lists three (`parted_uniq_tbl_i_key` + one per partition). (2) With the constraint deferred, PG accepts the duplicate INSERT and raises `23505` at **COMMIT**; goopg raised at the INSERT. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** Both goopg sites that build a partition's copy of the parent's UNIQUE/PK index cloned only the *shape* fields — `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go:4611` (`CREATE TABLE … PARTITION OF`) and `:8380` (`ALTER TABLE … ATTACH PARTITION`) each called `createBTreeIndex(…, parentIdx.Unique, parentIdx.Primary, parentIdx.NullsNotDistinct, parentIdx.ColDescending, parentIdx.ColNullsFirst)` and never forwarded `Deferrable`, `InitiallyDeferred`, `IsConstraint`. The two downstream consumers were already correct and are what proved the fix — `uniqueCheckDeferred` (`internal/executor/deferred_unique.go:51-53`) reads `idx.Deferrable`, `PGConstraintRowsForDBOid` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:6624-6627`) filters on `idx.IsConstraint`; neither was touched. Fix reuses the pre-existing `btreeIndexProps` optional-arg struct (`~:11624`, applied inside `createBTreeIndex`'s existing `if xp != nil` block at `~:11751` beside Fillfactor/Tablespace) rather than growing the positional signature; both sites forward identically. **Why PG cannot have this bug:** `postgres/src/backend/commands/indexcmds.c:DefineIndex` recurses on the *same* `IndexStmt*` per partition (dispatch at `indexcmds.c:706`), so the three fields propagate for free — PG has no per-field copy step to get wrong. This is an architectural divergence class, not a typo: expect the same shape wherever goopg clones a catalog object instead of re-running its constructor. Rule #2: the two clone sites ARE a genuine twin pair (confirmed by reading both); the column-level declared-UNIQUE path (`:4620`) and the plain-index inheritance loop (`:4631-4674`) are NOT affected. Guards: `internal/testport/partitioned_deferrable_unique_test.go` — `TestPort_PartitionedDeferrableUniqueDefersToCommitViaSetConstraintsAll` (FAIL-pre proven by stashing ONLY `operators_ddl.go`; pre-fix it fails at the INSERT), `TestPort_PartitionedUniqueConstraintFansOutToPgConstraint` (both creation paths — the ATTACH half proves the twin site), and a **negative** guard `TestPort_PartitionedNonDeferrableUniqueErrorsAtInsert` (non-deferrable must still error at INSERT time). Measured: `constraints` 1181 → **1164** lines (−17), hunks 33 → **33**. Design: §13 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. **The named `SET CONSTRAINTS <name>` form is a distinct second bug, split out as M0134-0005g below** (correctly escalated by the implementer rather than absorbed); one follow-up ledgered 2026-08-18.
  - [x] **M0134-0005g — named `SET CONSTRAINTS <name> DEFERRED` does not reach a partition's cloned constraint** — split out of 0005f, where it was pinned but deliberately not fixed. `BasicSession.SetConstraintsNamed` (`internal/executor/session.go:383`) populates a flat `map[string]bool` keyed by the name the user typed — the PARENT's (`parted_uniq_tbl_i_key`) — but the per-partition clone is auto-named `parted_uniq_tbl_1_i_key`, so `uniqueCheckDeferToCommit` (`internal/executor/deferred_unique.go:66`) looks up `sess.UniqueConstraintDeferred(idx.Name, …)` with the CHILD's name, misses, and silently falls back to `idx.InitiallyDeferred` (false) — the check runs at statement end instead of COMMIT. The unnamed `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL DEFERRED` form is unaffected (verified live in 0005f). PG oracle: `postgres/src/backend/commands/trigger.c:AlterConstraint` resolves the named constraint's OID then **walks `pg_constraint.conparentid`** to collect every descendant partition-child constraint OID before resolving deferred state; goopg has no `conparentid` equivalent — `catalog.Index` carries no "cloned from constraint X" linkage and `session.constraintDeferral` is partition-blind. **Scope note:** the same flat resolver serves `FKConstraintDeferred`, so FK constraints inherited onto partitions are very likely affected too — UNPROBED, probe before sizing. This is the sole surviving `parted_uniq` hunk in the diff (`@@ -590,13 +579,13 @@`, 5 matches) — it is this gap, NOT a psql transcript-echo artifact (goopg's ERROR precedes `COMMIT;` precisely because it errors at the INSERT). Design: §13.4 of the same doc. Needs a new constraint-hierarchy linkage — size it as more than one slice until a probe says otherwise. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** The §13.4 sizing ("needs a constraint-hierarchy linkage that does not exist anywhere in goopg today; strictly larger than one slice") was **REFUTED by a read-only probe before any code was written** — `catalog.Index.PartitionParentOID` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:1818`) plus `InMemory.indexPartitionChildren` (`:2369`) and `RegisterIndexPartitionChild`/`IndexPartitionChildren` (`:4744`/`:4752`) already existed and were already correctly wired at TWO sites (`operators_ddl.go:6846`, `:8610`); the two 0005f clone sites simply never assigned the field. No new `catalog.Index` field, no `conparentid` column, one slice. **The generalisable lesson:** §13.4 sized this by reading the *consumer* and the PG oracle and inferring the producer must be missing — it was merely unwired at two of four sites, so probe the producer before any "needs new infrastructure" claim. Fix: both clone sites (`operators_ddl.go` ~:4615 `PARTITION OF`, ~:8402 `ATTACH PARTITION` — the SAME Rule-#2 twin pair as 0005f, both changed) now set `childIdx.PartitionParentOID = parentIdx.OID` and call `RegisterIndexPartitionChild`, copying the already-correct idiom rather than inventing one; `uniqueCheckDeferToCommit` (`deferred_unique.go:66`) tries the index's OWN name first (so naming a partition's own constraint keeps working) then walks `PartitionParentOID` child→root against the session map, bounded by a new `maxPartitionParentWalk = 64` mirroring `maxCTIDChainWalk`. **Why goopg walks OIDs where PG scans names:** PG's `trigger.c:AlterConstraint` (~:5820-5960) resolves primarily by a `(conname, connamespace)` scan because PG **reuses the same `conname`** for a partition's clone (verified live on the 18.3 oracle), with a secondary `conparentid` descent; goopg names clones differently, so porting PG's by-name scan would find nothing — do NOT "fix" this by renaming the clones. Guards (extending 0005f's file, not a new one): `TestPort_PartitionedDeferrableUniqueDefersToCommitViaSetConstraintsNamed` (FAIL-pre proven by reverting only the two production files — pre-fix it fails at the INSERT with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "parted_uniq_tbl_1_i_key"`) and `TestPort_PartitionedDeferrableUniqueNamedChildOwnConstraintStillDefers` (a **regression** guard for the child-name-first ordering — it passes pre-fix by construction, and is recorded as such rather than passed off as evidence the fix works); the negative direction is already covered by 0005f's `TestPort_PartitionedNonDeferrableUniqueErrorsAtInsert`, re-run green not duplicated. **Measured: `parted_uniq` matches 5 → 0; aggregate UNCHANGED at 1164 lines / 33 hunks** — established as a true counterfactual (stash only the two production files, re-measure, restore), because the first post-fix-only reading wrongly concluded an earlier slice had already cleared the hunk. A single post-fix measurement cannot attribute a metric change. Design: §14 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. Two divergences deliberately not absorbed: **M0134-0005h** (below) and the `SET CONSTRAINTS` catalog-validation gap (ledgered).
  - [x] **M0134-0005h — deferred FK checks on PARTITIONED tables silently pass where PG raises 23503 (a SILENT INTEGRITY GAP, same class as 0005e)** — discovered by the M0134-0005g probe while checking whether the FK twin shared 0005g's name-mismatch bug. **It does not — the FK path is broken by a different and worse mechanism.** (1) goopg creates **no per-partition FK constraint clone at all**: `pg_constraint` shows 1 row where PG 18.3 shows 2 (and PG reuses the same `conname` on both). (2) `runAllDeferredFKChecks` / `fullTableFKCheck` (`internal/executor/operators_fk.go:430` / `:462`) resolve the FK owner **by name** and scan the **partitioned root**, which has zero physical blocks — every row lives in a leaf partition — so the COMMIT-tier recheck scans nothing, finds nothing, and the transaction **commits with a violated FK on disk**. Verified live on both engines: goopg's deferred partitioned-FK COMMIT succeeds silently; PG raises `23503`. A non-partitioned deferred FK works correctly on goopg (sanity control), so this is partition-specific. Wiring `PartitionParentOID` (the 0005g fix) does **not** address it. Resume point: make both functions walk partition leaves via `im.PartitionChildren(...)` instead of scanning the storage-less root; the per-partition `pg_constraint` FK clone rows (needed for introspection / pg_dump parity, not for enforcement) are separable and may be a second slice. PG oracle: `postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c` FK partition recursion + `ri_triggers.c`. Design: §14.6 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. **Prefer this over the remaining §11.4 list — it is a silent wrong-answer bug, not a message divergence.** **LANDED 2026-08-18.** §14.6's root cause was CONFIRMED by a live two-engine probe but its sizing was again too large — the third time in this milestone. Two refinements pinned before any code: (1) **what is queued is correct and must not change** — `DeferredFKCheck.ChildTableName` (`internal/executor/session.go:17`) records the ROOT, set at `operators_fk.go:129` from `fkOwnerTbl = o.plan.Table` (`operators_storage.go:2558`), matching how the two already-correct sibling scans work; the bug is entirely at SCAN time. (2) **The recursive walk already existed and was already used twice in the same file** — `allDescendants(im, tbl, snapEpoch)` (`operators_fk.go:950`, inheritance+partition BFS with detach-pending filtering) is called by `scanTableForMatch` (`:1000`) and `scanTableForMatchFKWait` (`:1370`) with `snapDetachEpoch(ctx)`. Only `fullTableFKCheck` (`:465`) scanned the single table object handed to it; a partitioned root has zero physical blocks, so its `NBlocks` loop ran **zero iterations** and the check "passed" by scanning nothing. So no `PartitionChildren` walk was written — both scans split into a per-relation helper (`fullTableFKCheckRel`, `validateFKConstraintExistingRowsRel`) that runs on the root then on every `allDescendants` descendant. **The DDL twin was live-probed BEFORE being touched, not assumed from code shape**, and reproduced independently (goopg accepted `ALTER TABLE fk_child ADD CONSTRAINT … FOREIGN KEY …` over a partitioned root holding a violating leaf row; PG raised 23503 naming `fk_child_p1`) — had it not reproduced it would have been ledgered, not edited. Two load-bearing details: columns are resolved **by NAME per relation** against each descendant's own `Columns`/`RelFileNode`, because a partition's column order can differ from its root's and hoisting a root-computed positional index would have been a fresh silent-corruption bug; and `assertParentExists`'s `reportTbl` now carries the scanned leaf (it was self-referential to the root), which is what makes the message name the leaf — verified byte-for-byte against the live 18.3 oracle rather than inferred from `ri_triggers.c`. Non-`*catalog.InMemory` catalogs fall back to root-only, guarded exactly as `scanTableForMatch` guards it. **Why PG cannot have this bug:** it installs per-leaf RI triggers (`tablecmds.c` FK partition recursion + `ri_triggers.c`), so its deferred after-trigger queue holds LEAF entries natively and has no "scan the owner" step to get wrong; goopg's one root-scoped queue entry is a legitimate design difference that makes leaf expansion the scan's own responsibility. Guards: `internal/testport/partitioned_deferred_fk_test.go`, 7 `TestPort_*` — COMMIT-tier positive, DDL-twin positive, a **multi-level** case (`fk_child_p1_p1`, proving `allDescendants` recurses), a satisfied-FK negative guard (partitioned + deferred must still commit cleanly) and the non-partitioned control (must not regress). All three positive guards FAIL-pre/PASS-post by stashing only the two production files, each failing with `expected 23503 … got <nil>` — **the pre-fix behaviour was a silent SUCCESS**, which is precisely why no message-diff guard would ever have caught this and why the negative guards were mandatory. **Measured: `constraints` 1164 lines / 33 hunks, UNCHANGED — and that is the honest result, not a disappointment**: this bug was found by the 0005g *probe*, not by the `constraints` case, which never exercises a partitioned deferred FK. The slice's value is the closed integrity gap, not a line delta; booking it as a diff win would have been the same false attribution §14 already made once in the other direction. Gates: units suite PASS, `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35 — Rule #1), the FK/partition testport set PASS including 13 pre-existing FK/partition isolation specs. Design: §15 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. Three follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18 (no per-partition `pg_constraint` FK clone row — introspection/pg_dump parity only, enforcement is now correct without it; `fkCascadeDelete` walks one level where `allDescendants` recurses, found by reading and **not** live-probed; `cloneAndValidateAttachPartitionFKs` probed and found NOT affected, recorded as a negative result so it is not re-probed).
  - [x] **M0134-0005i — `ALTER TABLE … SET NOT NULL` / `ADD CONSTRAINT … NOT NULL` never cascaded to EXISTING children** — chosen from a fresh measured bucket census (probe `tmp/ralph-handoffs/m0134-0005i-probe`), not from §11.4's stale guesses: at 1164 lines the dominant driver was **~690 lines** of NOT-NULL inheritance, and the previous loop's second hypothesis (`COMMENT ON CONSTRAINT … does not exist`) was **REFUTED as an independent bug** — it was a downstream symptom of the same missing cascade. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** Both handlers (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go` `case parser.AlterTableSetNotNull:` ~:9661 and `case parser.AlterTableAddNotNull:` ~:9754) mutated **only** the table named in the ALTER; children silently kept accepting NULLs. Machinery was unwired, not missing — the fourth time in this milestone: the sibling **DROP CONSTRAINT** path (~:10917-10960) already cascades, and `CREATE TABLE … INHERITS` (~:3925-3977) already seeds children with the merge accounting. Fix = one shared `cascadeNotNullToChildren` (+ `maxNotNullCascadeDepth = 64`) called from **both** handlers — PG 18 implements both spellings in the single `postgres/src/backend/commands/tablecmds.c:7913 ATExecSetNotNull`, so two divergent goopg implementations would have been a fresh Rule-#2 defect. Three decisions taken before briefing and all held: **recursive, not one level** (PG re-invokes itself per child at `:8062-8079`, so grandchildren are reached — the DROP sibling's one-level walk is a ledgered defect class, deliberately not copied); **column resolved BY NAME per child** (a child's column order can differ from its parent's — the §15.3 lesson); **merge, do not duplicate** (`:7971-7993` increments `coninhcount` on an existing child row). Guards: 6 `TestPort_*` in `internal/testport/notnull_inherit_cascade_test.go`, incl. a grandchild case proving recursion and a merge case asserting exactly one `pg_constraint` row; the three positive guards are FAIL-pre/PASS-post by stashing only `operators_ddl.go`, and **each pre-fix failure is a silent SUCCESS** (`INSERT … VALUES (NULL) succeeded … want 23502`), which is why the negative guards were mandatory. Measured **1164 → 1122 lines (−42)**, hunks 33 → 33, as a true stash-based counterfactual. Gates: units suite PASS (7m46s), `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35 — Rule #1), `TestPort_.*(NotNull|Inherit)` PASS (7 subtests). Design: §16 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. Three follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18 — **the successor slice is bucket 2: `DEFAULT -1 * currval('insert_seq')` silently evaluates to NULL, corrupting SELECT output AND letting `CHECK (x+z=0)` accept violating rows via 3-valued logic (~150-180 lines, a silent wrong answer).**
  - [x] **M0134-0005j — a column omitted from an EXPLICIT INSERT column list lost its DEFAULT** — bucket 2 of the §16.1 measured census, the largest remaining *silent wrong-answer* bucket. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** `INSERT INTO INSERT_TBL(y) VALUES('Y')` left `z INT DEFAULT -1 * currval('insert_seq')` **NULL with no error**, which corrupted SELECT output *and* let `CHECK (x + z = 0)` accept the violating row — a NULL predicate passes, PG rejects only FALSE (`postgres/src/backend/executor/execMain.c:ExecConstraints`). Root cause was a **planner gap, not an evaluator gap**: goopg has TWO default-expression evaluators, and the crippled one is reached for exactly one statement shape. `rewriteInsertDefaultMarkers` (`internal/optimizer/planner.go` ~:9876) padded omitted columns only when `len(s.Columns)==0`; with an explicit column list the omitted column never entered the target list, so `insertOp` patched it at run time with the hand-rolled mini-AST-walker `applyDefaultsForMissing`/`evalGenExpr` (`internal/executor/operators_generated.go`), whose `evalGenFuncCall` (~:193-240) special-cases only `nextval` and **falls through to `return NullDatum, nil` — silently — for every other function call** (~:239). Design decision taken before briefing and explicitly recorded as the point of the slice: **fix the planner, do NOT add `currval` to the mini-evaluator** — the ~10-line patch would have left a second evaluator that silently NULLs the *next* unhandled builtin, this project's Rule-#2 sibling-drift trap in its purest form. Extending the plan-time substitution retires the mini-evaluator for **both** INSERT twins (`operators_storage.go:2440` plain, `operators_upsert.go:213` ON CONFLICT — fixed by construction, each proven by its own end-to-end test) and fixes the secondary `nextval`-bypasses-`ctx.CurrSeqVals` off-by-one for free (the inline `s.nextVal()` never wrote the map `evalCurrval` reads, so `currval` returned 7 where PG returns 8). The briefed **serial double-advance hazard did not materialise** — a serial column's catalog `DefaultExpr` is nil, so it is excluded by construction; probed pre- and post-fix on a live capped server. Refuted en route: goopg's CHECK evaluation is already PG-correct (`internal/executor/operators_fk.go:1699` `checkConstraints` `continue`s on NULL), so the accepted-violating-row symptom was purely downstream. Guards: 4 plan-shape tests in `internal/optimizer/planner_test.go` + 5 end-to-end in the new `internal/executor/default_omitted_column_test.go` (both twins, the currval off-by-one, the serial-hazard guard, and a negative guard that a no-DEFAULT omitted column stays NULL / still raises NOT NULL) — all FAIL-pre/PASS-post by stashing only `planner.go`. Measured **1122 → 1024 lines (−98), hunks 33 → 31**. Gates: `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/optimizer/ ./internal/executor/`, units suite PASS, `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (**Q12=2, Q13=35** — Rule #1), `TestPort_.*(Insert|Default|Copy|Sequence)` PASS. Design: §17 of `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`. Three follow-ups ledgered 2026-08-18 — **`INSERT … SELECT` with an explicit column list is still on the old path** (the `s.Select != nil` early return), the **apply worker** (`applyworker.go:286`) is the last live caller keeping the mini-evaluator alive, and **`COPY` with a partial column list drops every DEFAULT** (`copy.go:323` never calls the filler at all).
      **Measured + bucketed 2026-08-18** — full map in `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md`
      (baseline 1496 lines / 30 hunks, post-C19 harness fix; **never compare to a
      pre-2026-08-18 `constraints` number**). Seven buckets, one cascade.
      **Bucket 1 LANDED**: `PREPARE`'s parameter-type validator `isValidSQLTypeName`
      (`internal/postmaster/dispatch.go:2038`) was a hand-written allowlist of *bare*
      built-in names, so `PREPARE p (regclass[])` failed 42704 and 13 of 30 hunks were
      the follow-on `prepared statement "get_nnconstraint_info" does not exist`. It now
      strips balanced typmod groups and `[]`/`[N]` array suffixes and accepts the `reg*`
      family plus the other built-ins goopg already implements. Cascade 13 → **0**;
      diff 1496 → **1515** lines / 30 hunks — it *grew* because the fix **unmasks**
      (aborted statements now emit real, still-wrong result sets), so diff line count is
      not this case's metric of record.
      **Bucket 2 LANDED 2026-08-18**: `checkConstraints`
      (`internal/executor/operators_fk.go:1664`) — the single runtime fan-in for
      INSERT/COPY/UPDATE — now skips a check whose `tbl.NamedChecks[i].NotEnforced` is
      set, mirroring PG `execMain.c:ExecRelCheck:1813-1815`. The two slices are
      index-aligned 1:1 by construction (`catalog.AddCheckFull`,
      `internal/catalog/catalog.go:288`); the ADD-CONSTRAINT scan and VALIDATE
      CONSTRAINT paths already honoured the flag and were left alone. Diff 1515 →
      **1465** lines (hunks 30 → 31: one surviving hunk split, not new divergence);
      `NE_CHECK_TBL`/`NE_INSERT_TBL_CON` hunks gone. Pinned by
      `TestCheckConstraintNotEnforcedSkipsRuntimeEvaluation`. `NOT ENFORCED` on
      **UNIQUE** constraints still diverges — ledgered, different code path.
      **Bucket 3 LANDED 2026-08-18**: `DROP CONSTRAINT` / `RENAME CONSTRAINT` now resolve
      named NOT NULL constraints. The Rule-#2 sibling check came back **positive** — the
      RENAME path is an inline `case parser.AlterTableRenameConstraint:`
      (`operators_ddl.go:8796`) that already *referenced* `NotNullConstraints` in its
      `constraintNameInUse` helper while its own resolution chain skipped them. Child
      recursion matches by **column name**, not constraint name (`tablecmds.c:14251-14255`).
      Diff 1465 → **1431** lines (hunks 31 → 33 — an unmasking).
      **Bucket 6 LANDED 2026-08-18** — and its premise was wrong: `ALTER CONSTRAINT` was
      never a blanket parse error (`[NOT] DEFERRABLE` / `[NOT] ENFORCED` already worked).
      Only `NOT VALID` (parser-only — PG raises it in the **grammar action**,
      `gram.y:2672-2676`, `0A000`) and `[NO] INHERIT` (both arms; bare `INHERIT` is its own
      production `gram.y:2686-2699`, `NO INHERIT` rides `ConstraintAttributeSpec`) were
      broken. Executor arm mirrors `tablecmds.c:12615-12684` (contype-gated to
      `CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL`, no-op if already in state, **one-level** child propagation).
      Sibling check **negative** this time. Diff 1431 → **1411** lines, hunks 33 → **33**.
      **The flat hunk count is the finding**: both target statements now behave, but every
      hunk holding them stays open on an unrelated defect — the **bucket-interference**
      signature (2 ledger rows).
      **Next slices, re-ranked by that measurement:** (i) the `EXECUTE
      get_nnconstraint_info(…)` → `(0 rows)` **file-wide masking bug** — cheapest, and it
      unblocks the observability of several buckets at once; (ii) Bucket 3's leftover NOT
      NULL inheritance-propagation gap (`operators_ddl.go:4748-4753`). **Not slices:**
      Bucket 4 (statement-level/deferred UNIQUE checking) is now known to be what caps both
      Bucket 6 *and* Bucket 2's remaining hunks — goopg builds the unique index immediately
      for `UNIQUE … DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`, so `unique_tbl_i_key` never exists —
      but it is still milestone-sized and needs its own research pass; Bucket 5 (missing
      GiST `circle_ops` default opclass) likewise. **Do not brief Bucket 7** (float8
      `DEFAULT` truncation on an omitted column, `a_expr`-vs-`b_expr` DEFAULT grammar,
      `currval()` default ordering) — root cause is not pinned; it needs another research
      pass on the missing-column catalog-default fill path.
  - [x] **M0134-0005k — CREATE TABLE had NO not-null constraint-name / `NO INHERIT` conflict validation** — bucket 1 of the §18.1 re-measured census (~49% of the remaining diff) and a **silent-accept** gap: PG rejects all 14 `notnull_tbl_fail` shapes (`postgres/src/test/regress/sql/constraints.sql:667-680`); goopg accepted every one, corrupting catalog state and cascading into `relation already exists` + phantom-column noise downstream. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** Mirrors PG's TWO passes — column-level `parse_utilcmd.c:transformColumnDefinition` (plural wording) and table-level `heap.c:AddRelationNotNullConstraints` (singular wording); the singular/plural split is load-bearing, not cosmetic. New parser support for the table-constraint form `[CONSTRAINT name] NOT NULL col [NO INHERIT]` (previously parsed then silently dropped). **14/14** byte-exact, diff **1024 → 933** lines. Exposed and fixed three pre-existing bugs, incl. INHERITS copying NOT NULL to children while ignoring the parent's NO INHERIT flag. The catalog model needed no extension. Design: `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md` §18. Ledgered: ADD COLUMN registers no `pg_constraint` row for an inline named NOT NULL, `ALTER TABLE … INHERIT` not-null validation, and inherited `coninhcount`/`conislocal` accounting.
  - [x] **M0134-0005l — `COPY FROM` enforced NO constraints at all (a SILENT INTEGRITY GAP, same class as 0005e/0005h)** — `insertSourceRow` (`internal/executor/copy.go`) scattered the parsed columns into a `Row`, left the rest NULL, and handed it straight to the raw heap writer `writeHeapRowReturning`: no column DEFAULTs, no NOT NULL, no CHECK, no domain constraints. goopg durably stored rows PG rejects outright. PG forks nothing by statement shape — `copyfrom.c:CopyFrom` (`:1352-1358`) calls `ExecConstraints` unconditionally per row and builds `defmap`/`defexprs` (~`:1545-1833`) for every column absent from the column list. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** The fix is pure Rule-#2 wiring, not new logic: `insertSourceRow` now runs `insertOp.Next`'s exact sequence (`applyDefaultsForMissing` → NOT NULL loop → `checkConstraints` → `checkDomainConstraintsForRow` — the ORDER is load-bearing, PG's error precedence depends on it) before the heap write. `missing[]` mirrors PG's `defmap` (a column *in* the list holding an explicit NULL is NOT missing) and `needsConstraints` is a per-statement fast path so bulk loads into constraint-free tables pay nothing — a genuine fast path, since `DeclaredTypeName` is set only for domain-typed columns. **Census correction worth keeping:** the carried-over framing ("COPY with a *partial* column list") was wrong — the fixture (`constraints.sql:255-267`) is a *bare* `COPY COPY_TBL FROM` with no column list, so the defect was unconditional; re-derive a carried-over candidate's framing from its fixture before briefing it. Measured: `constraints` **933 → 923** lines (31 hunks). Guards: `TestCopyFrom{CheckConstraintViolationRejected,DefaultFilledForOmittedColumn,NotNullViolationRejected,ConstraintFreeTableFastPath}` in `internal/executor/copy_constraints_test.go` (first three FAIL-pre/PASS-post). Design: `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md` §19. Two deferrals ledgered 2026-08-18: **COPY BINARY (`PushBinaryData`) still bypasses the whole sequence**, and COPY FROM's 9 error sites drop `DETAIL:`/`CONTEXT:` (why the target hunk shrank rather than vanished).
  - [x] **M0134-0005m — `INSERT … SELECT` silently dropped omitted columns' DEFAULT expressions** — `INSERT INTO INSERT_TBL(y) SELECT yd FROM tmp` left `z INT DEFAULT -1 * currval('insert_seq')` NULL where PG fills it (`constraints.diff:110-166`, 3 hunks, the third pure downstream corruption of the second); the no-column-list narrower-SELECT form (`INSERT INTO INSERT_TBL SELECT * FROM tmp`) had the same hole. **LANDED 2026-08-18.** **The obvious diagnosis was the wrong half:** `rewriteInsertDefaultMarkers` does bail at `planner.go:9877` on `s.Select != nil`, but the DEFAULT was still *attempted* — the executor derives `insertMissing` purely from "ordinal not in `ColumnIndex`" (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go:2405-2440`) and routed the column to `applyDefaultsForMissing`, whose deliberately lightweight `evalGenExpr`/`evalGenFuncCall` (`operators_generated.go:101-230`) has **no `currval` case** and silently returns `NullDatum, nil`. Fix is therefore planner-side and routes the DEFAULT through the FULL evaluator: `planInsert`'s SELECT branch wraps the planned SELECT in an `optimizer.Project` passing its own columns through as `ColumnRef`s and appending one `resolveExpr`-resolved `DefaultExpr` per omitted eligible column, extending `colIndex` in lockstep — **the executor needed zero change** (`Insert.Next` is already generic over `ColumnIndex` width). Rule-#2 twin pair closed by factoring the eligibility predicate into shared `defaultAppendableColumns` (`DefaultExpr != nil && !GeneratedAlways`, skip present), called by both the VALUES marker rewriter and the new SELECT path — the predicate is what keeps serial/identity columns on `autoGenerateSerialValues` and avoids a double sequence advance. PG oracle: `rewriteHandler.c:rewriteTargetListIU` (~`:775`) from `RewriteQuery` (~`:4046-4070`) — PG runs ONE INSERT rewrite path regardless of source shape; the only branch there is a VALUES-RTE *optimization*, not a semantic fork. Guards: `internal/executor/default_omitted_column_select_test.go` (currval fixture, no-column-list narrower SELECT, explicit-NULL-beats-DEFAULT, serial-no-double-advance) plus `TestPlanInsertSelectFewerColumns{AppendsOmittedDefault,TruncatesUndefaultedColumn}` — the latter re-added after the rename, since the original was the sole guard for the 0118-0038 panic regression. **Assertion lesson:** `SELECT 1, 2` already plans to a `*Project` on its own, so "Source is/isn't a `*Project`" tests nothing; assert `len(ins.Source.Output())` instead. Design: `docs/design/0134-0005-constraints-sql-divergence.md` §20. Deferral ledgered 2026-08-18 (COPY FROM / upsert reach the same currval-blind evaluator).
